# Incremental checkpoints

**Evidence sources.** The measurements this design cites come from a laptop and from AWS. "Measurements" holds runs on a laptop on 2026-11-05 against a copy of today's archive code. "Benchmarks" holds runs on AWS on 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-06, made with the *harness*, a standalone program that implements both today's checkpoint method and this design's capture, storage, and restore paths and runs no Lenny component. Its "Kubernetes checks" ran the same builds on a single-node cluster. A pause written as *walk / tracker* gives the figure with the stat walk and with the change tracker as the change source. "Benchmark terms" under "Terms" defines the scenarios and configurations the tables name. "Session isolation spikes" holds three local runs of 2026-11-12 that checked this design against the session isolation layout and its file-descriptor broker. "Re-validation through the broker" holds two AWS runs of 2026-11-12, made with a new standalone harness that implements this design's passes through a patched copy of the C supervisor and its broker on the session isolation layout: a re-validation of "Benchmarks" through the broker, and a run of Kata `block-plain` with a dedicated `fsGroup` together with the parallel stat walk. The figures of "Benchmarks" predate the broker and stand as measured, and "Benchmarks", "Figures the broker can change" sets each of them beside its measured value through the broker.

## Problem

[§4.4 Event / Checkpoint Store](../../spec/04_system-components.md#44-event--checkpoint-store) archives a session's whole workspace into one gzip-compressed tar stream on every checkpoint. A quiescing runtime, which pauses itself on request so that the checkpoint is consistent, stays quiesced until the last byte of that stream is uploaded. Both the cost of a checkpoint and the length of the pause grow with the size of the workspace.

- **The pause grows with the workspace.** The adapter, Lenny's container in the agent pod that runs the checkpoint, sends `checkpoint_request` (see "Quiesce handshake" under "Terms"), archives and uploads every byte, and sends `checkpoint_complete` after the stream ends (`Checkpoint` in `pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go`). [§4.4.3 Checkpoint Quiescence, Timeout, and Abort Handling](../../spec/04_system-components.md#443-checkpoint-quiescence-timeout-and-abort-handling) states the relationship as `expected_quiescence_seconds ≈ workspace_bytes / (100 × 1024 × 1024)`.
- **A large workspace never completes a checkpoint.** A periodic attempt runs under the 60-second `CheckpointTimeout` (`attemptContext` in `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go`). A workspace that cannot be archived and uploaded in 60 seconds fails every attempt. Each failed attempt still pauses a quiescing runtime for up to 60 seconds, and the attempt repeats every `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds`, the gateway-wide checkpoint interval.
- **Every attempt starts over.** A tar stream has no reuse between attempts. A session holding a 5 GiB clone uploads 5 GiB on each checkpoint, even when one file changed. The tar chunks a timed-out attempt uploaded are a prefix of a tar stream that the next attempt cannot use.
- **The size limit replaces one failure with another.** [§4.4.4 Checkpoint SLOs and Scheduling](../../spec/04_system-components.md#444-checkpoint-slos-and-scheduling) caps the workspace at the pool's `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`, 512 MiB by default, to bound the pause. Under that cap, a session that clones a large monorepo would be evicted by the size limit of the workspace's `emptyDir` volume (`emptyDir.sizeLimit`), or would run without checkpoints because the adapter's size probe before each attempt rejects every attempt. The tree implements neither limit by default (see "Defects found while verifying"), so such a session meets the 60-second `CheckpointTimeout` of the preceding bullet.
- **Best-effort checkpoints fail when files change.** The spec describes a checkpoint of a Basic-level or Standard-level runtime, which does not pause, as a snapshot with minor inconsistencies. The archive code fails the whole attempt when a file grows during the archive or disappears between the directory walk and the read (see "Measurements").
- **Interrupts wait for uploads.** The Checkpoint handler holds the pod-wide operation lock, which serializes a checkpoint with an interrupt, from before the size probe until the upload ends (`pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go`, `s.ops.Begin`), so an interrupt waits for the whole upload on every integration level.

## Scope

This design covers how the adapter captures a workspace, the stored format, the upload and restore protocol, derive, retention, garbage collection, and the runtime's quiesce contract, for checkpoints and for the seal at session end. It also covers the Runtime and pool settings that turn checkpointing off and set the checkpoint interval, the order in which a pod's slots checkpoint (IC-1, "Staggering across slots"), and the removal of the embedded adapter's `SIGSTOP` checkpoint path (IC-9). It applies to every isolation profile and to every runtime, whichever capabilities it declares, and to every pod, a pod that serves one session and a pod with several slots alike, with one implementation. The adapter reaches a session's files through the file-descriptor broker of [`0145-session-isolation`](../0145-session-isolation/change.md), and this design states how each pass uses it. A session's checkpointed state is its `/workspace` and its `/session` directory, the two checkpointed paths of [`0118-agent-pod-layout`, "PL-3"](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout).

The event store, the transcript, and the eviction minimal-state record in [§4.4.5 Checkpoint Storage Failure and Eviction Fallback](../../spec/04_system-components.md#445-checkpoint-storage-failure-and-eviction-fallback) are unchanged. Node-level filesystem snapshots are evaluated and deferred (see "Node-level snapshots").

## Terms

| Term | Meaning |
| :-- | :-- |
| Current method | Today's checkpoint path. `ArchiveTree` archives the whole workspace into one gzip-compressed tar stream, and the adapter uploads the stream in fixed-size tar chunks while a quiescing runtime stays quiesced. See "Current state", and "Tar chunk and partial manifest" below. |
| Snapshot | One stored capture of a session's workspace and session directory under this design: a manifest and the packs it references, recorded by a snapshot row in Postgres. A checkpoint or a seal produces one. |
| Session's trees | The two checkpointed directories of a slot: `/workspace`, the session's working tree, and `/session`, the runtime's private per-session state, such as a harness's transcripts ([PL-3](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)). Each is a disk-backed volume of its own. A snapshot holds both, and its manifest keeps them apart (IC-2, "Manifests"). |
| Quiescing runtime | A runtime that declares the `checkpoint` capability of [`0139-runtime-protocol`, "RP-2"](../0139-runtime-protocol/change.md#rp-2-capabilities-and-their-dependencies). It pauses itself on `checkpoint_request` and answers `checkpoint_ready`. Every other runtime is captured on the best-effort path while it runs. |
| Seal | The snapshot taken at session end, which becomes the session's `final_workspace_ref`. See IC-5, "Seal". |
| Pre-copy pass | The first pass of a checkpoint. It reads every file that has no trusted entry in the file index and uploads their new chunks while the runtime keeps running. See IC-1, "Pre-copy". |
| Capture pass | The second pass. It finds the files changed since pre-copy and reads or clones them into the spool. A quiescing runtime is quiesced during this pass and no other. See IC-1, "Capture". |
| Upload pass | The third pass. After the runtime resumes, it packs the spooled bytes, uploads them, and asks the gateway to finalize the snapshot. See IC-1, "Upload". |
| Pause | The time a quiescing runtime stays quiesced for one checkpoint: the handshake, the change source, and reading or cloning the changed files. |
| Pause budget | The pool's `checkpointPauseBudgetSeconds`, the time the capture pass may take. Before it sends `checkpoint_request` the adapter estimates the capture time from the slowest rates it has observed, and it pauses the runtime only when the estimate times `pauseEstimateMargin`, a constant of 1.5, fits the budget. It ends a capture that runs past the budget. See IC-1, "Pause budget and convergence". |
| Pre-copy round and convergence | A pre-copy round is one pre-copy pass followed by a new estimate. Convergence is running further rounds, up to the pool's `checkpointPreCopyRounds`, until the estimate fits the pause budget. Live migration of a virtual machine works the same way: it copies memory while the machine runs and pauses the machine only to copy the last changed pages. |
| Changed set | The files the capture pass reads: files whose identity differs from the file index, dirty files, and racy entries. |
| File identity | What `fstat` reports for a file on a descriptor the file-descriptor broker passed, and this design compares: device, inode, size, `mtime` and `ctime` in nanoseconds, and mode. Identities are compared only among broker-passed descriptors, because their device and mount ID differ from the adapter's own view of the volume. See IC-1, "Reading the slot's files". |
| Link group | The names of one inode whose link count is above 1. The manifest records the group, so a restore recreates its names as hard links, and the tracker marks every name of the group dirty when one is written. |
| Metadata-only entry | An entry that the slot's owner cannot read or list, such as a file of mode 0000 or a directory of mode 0300. The manifest records its metadata with the flag `metadata_only` and no content. See IC-1, "Reading the slot's files". |
| File index | The adapter's record of each file's identity and chunk list from the session's previous snapshot. A file whose identity matches a trusted entry is not read again. |
| Trusted entry | A file index entry whose identity matches the file's current identity and that is not a racy entry. Pre-copy keeps a trusted entry's chunk list without reading the file. See IC-1, "Change detection". |
| Dirty file | A file whose identity changed while pre-copy read it, which the capture pass reads again, or a path the change tracker reported. |
| Racy entry | An index entry whose file `mtime` falls within `racyWindow` before the read that produced the entry started. The capture pass reads it again, following git's rule for racily clean index entries. See IC-1, "Change detection". |
| Change source | How the capture pass learns which files changed: the stat walk or the change tracker. |
| Stat walk | A parallel walk of the session's trees that reads every entry's identity and reads no content. The adapter lists each directory with `getdents` on a directory descriptor the file-descriptor broker passed, or that the adapter opened beneath such a descriptor, and calls `statx` on each name relative to that descriptor from several threads. It falls back to the broker's `statat` for a directory the adapter may not search. Its cost grows with the number of entries. "The walk" means this walk. See IC-1, "The walk". |
| Change tracker | An inotify instance that the slot's runtime supervisor creates and adds watches to on the adapter's request, and whose events the adapter reads from the shared descriptor. It records which paths changed since the last capture, so that the capture pass does not walk. "The tracker" means this watcher. See IC-1, "Change tracker". |
| Barrier file and swap | At the capture, the adapter writes one byte to a barrier file that the file-descriptor broker created in the session's tree, and the tracker reads events until it sees the barrier file's event, returns its dirty set, and starts an empty one. That exchange is the swap, and the time the adapter waits for it is the barrier wait. The barrier file is unrelated to the gateway's CheckpointBarrier protocol. |
| Degraded tracker | A tracker that failed to add a watch or missed a change that the pre-copy cross-check found. Every capture walks until the adapter restarts it. A tracker whose queue overflowed walks at every capture until the next walk completes (IC-1, "Change tracker"). |
| Event cap | The adapter's bound of 16,384 events read in one swap interval. Reaching it counts as an overflow, because gVisor's inotify queue has no bound and never reports `IN_Q_OVERFLOW` (IC-1, "Change tracker"). |
| Spool | The directory `/adapter/spool/<n>/checkpoint/` of slot `n` on the spool volume, where the capture pass places cloned or copied files and new chunks until the upload pass packs them. The spool volume is an `emptyDir` that only the adapter mounts (see "Spool volume" under "Terms from other designs and the platform"). |
| Spool cap | The pool's `checkpointCaptureBudgetBytes`, 512 MiB by default, which caps one session's disk use on the spool volume: its spool, its file index, and its output-file export clones under `/adapter/spool/<n>/export/` (IC-7). The adapter reserves it for each slot out of the spool's usable share, so a slot's checkpoint never waits for another slot's staging. Export clones, those of parts waiting for the export cap included, may use at most `exportSpoolFraction` of it, a constant of 0.5, which is 256 MiB at the default. The adapter counts each file at its logical size, or at its allocated size when that is larger, and never by `statfs`, because the kubelet counts a reflink clone at its full logical size. A single clone or copy is bounded by the session's remaining share of the cap. `CheckpointStart` carries the cap as `capture_budget_bytes`. |
| Spool size, usable share, and spool headroom | The spool volume's `sizeLimit` is the pool's `spoolSizeLimitBytes`, 16 GiB by default, one value for the whole pod ([`0153-workspace-materialization`, decision O9](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#open-decisions)). The adapter uses at most its usable share, `spoolSizeLimitBytes` divided by `spoolHeadroomFactor`, a constant of 2, which is 8 GiB at the default. The rest is the spool headroom, which keeps the kubelet's count of the volume below its limit when the kubelet counts more than the adapter (IC-7, "Spool cap"). Within the usable share the adapter reserves each slot's spool cap, and the remainder is the staging space the slots share for upload staging. |
| Size probe and reflink probe | The size probe is the adapter's measurement of the session's trees before each attempt, which it reports in `CheckpointProbe` and which refuses an attempt over `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`. The reflink probe is the adapter's check at start-up, for each pair of a slot's workspace volume and the spool volume, of how a slot file reaches the spool: by `FICLONE` with shared blocks (reflink mode), by `copy_file_range` as a full copy (copy mode), or only by reading (read mode). See IC-1, "Spool". |
| Reflink clone | A copy that shares the source file's disk blocks, made with the `FICLONE` ioctl, as `cp --reflink` does. It needs no privilege on XFS with `reflink=1` and on Btrfs, and its time does not grow with the file's size. |
| Chunk and chunk ID | A piece of a file cut by content-defined chunking (FastCDC), named by the SHA-256 of its uncompressed bytes. See IC-2. |
| Pack and pack ID | An object in the object store that holds many compressed chunks and a footer listing them, named by the SHA-256 of its bytes. The footer carries the pack nonce, a random 128-bit value the adapter writes into every pack it builds, so no two packs an adapter builds share a pack ID. The catalog identifies a pack by its object key, `(tenant_id, owner_session_id, pack_id)`, because a pod chooses the bytes it declares, so two sessions that declare identical bytes hold two packs. See IC-2. |
| Manifest | The sorted list of a snapshot's entries, one per path, with each file's chunk IDs. It is itself chunked and stored in packs. |
| Dedup set | The chunks the adapter treats as already stored, which it references instead of uploading. It is built from the same sources as the allowed set, and a chunk enters it only after the gateway confirms the chunk's pack with `PackCommitted`, or when the chunk belongs to an eligible source's platform chunk set. Checkpoints and exports of a session share it. |
| Allowed set and reference closure | The allowed set is the packs a session may reference in a new snapshot, the packs of its eligible sources' platform chunk sets included. The reference closure check is the gateway's refusal of a snapshot that references a pack outside that set. See IC-4. |
| Platform source and platform chunk set | A platform source is a warm repository store, identified by its owner and normalized URL, or a runtime's shared assets of type `artifact` and `inline`, identified by the runtime, or by the pod-set key of a derived runtime with a `sharedAssets` append, platform-derived or tenant-derived. Its platform chunk set is the chunks of every file the platform staged from it on a never-launched pod: the working trees of the store's warm-up checkouts without `.git`, or the asset files, and for a tenant-derived runtime's assets also the chunks of its uploaded artifacts, which the upload route writes. The set's packs hold that source's chunks only. See IC-4, "Platform chunk sets". |
| Never-launched pod | A pod to which no session has bound on any slot ([SIR-38](../0145-session-isolation/change.md#requirements)). A bind runs the session's materialization and setup commands before the runtime's launch, so the first bind, which precedes the first launch, ends it. The adapter records the pod's first bind, and the gateway's record of the pod shows it. |
| Contribution | One upload to a platform chunk set by a never-launched pod, recording the chunk list of every file it read. The newest contribution is the source's latest content. See IC-4, "Platform chunk sets". |
| Eligible source | A platform source whose chunks a session may reference: a store in the session's `coveredStores`, which holds the stores whose read checks the session passed and the public platform entries, or an assets source of the session's runtime: its own, and for a derived runtime with a `sharedAssets` append its base's as well, because the derived runtime's assets append to the base's. The gateway computes the list at every checkpoint and export. See IC-4, "Platform chunk sets". |
| Pack lease | A pack whose upload the gateway confirmed and that no snapshot or output file references yet is in state `unreferenced`, and it is kept until its lease expires, so that a later attempt or export can reuse its chunks. The lease length is the gateway setting `packLeaseSeconds`, 1800 seconds by default, which pool admission requires to be at least twice the larger of `checkpointAttemptTimeoutSeconds` and `exportTimeoutSeconds`, and each reference to the pack extends it. See IC-4, "Catalog". |
| "Already stored" answer | The gateway's answer `PackStored` to a `PackReady` whose pack is already in the session's allowed set. It covers the adapter's resend of a pack it built, after a `PackCommitted` the stream lost. The adapter skips that upload and references the pack. See IC-3. |
| Export queue | The file parts of a session that wait because `files.maxConcurrentExports` exports already run, at most `files.maxQueuedExports`, 8 by default ([MD-18](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#md-18-files)). |
| Export record | The `output_file_exports` row the gateway writes for each file part, keyed by session and part, which holds the export's state, its clocks, its quota reservation, and the coordination generation that drives it. Every update of the row carries that generation as a guard. See IC-4, "Catalog". |
| Unopened part | A file part that the adapter relays with `unopened: true` because it already holds `files.maxQueuedExports` plus `files.maxConcurrentExports` open export descriptors for the session. The gateway refuses exactly such parts with `too_many_exports` ([MD-18](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#md-18-files)). See IC-3. |
| `ExportAbort` | The message with which the gateway ends a failing export on the `ExportOutputFile` stream, with a reason, in place of `CheckpointAbort`. See IC-3. |
| Seal window | A session's effective `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds`: its runtime revision's value when set, and otherwise its pool's, 300 seconds by default and at most the chart value `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationCeilingSeconds`, 600 seconds by default. The seal checkpoint and the session's exports still running share it ([MD-18](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#md-18-files), A19). See IC-5, "Seal". |
| Best-effort capture | A capture that runs while the runtime keeps running. It tolerates files changing and tags the snapshot `consistency: best-effort`. A quiesced capture tags it `consistency: consistent`. See IC-1, "Best-effort capture". |
| Torn file | A file that kept changing through every re-read of a best-effort capture. It is stored as last read and flagged `torn` in the manifest. |
| Compaction | Writing the live chunks of a sparsely used pack into new packs, so that the old pack loses its last reference. See IC-6. |
| Checkpoint policy | `checkpointPolicy` on a Runtime or a pool: `full`, `sealOnly`, or `none`. The effective policy is the stricter of the two values. See IC-8. |
| Effective interval | The checkpoint interval a session runs on, resolved from the pool, the Runtime, and the gateway default and clamped to the deployer's bounds. See IC-8, "Interval". |
| Checkpoint stagger | The adapter's rule that at most one slot of a pod runs a checkpoint attempt at a time, and the others wait in a queue. See IC-1, "Staggering across slots". |
| Large-directory rules | The bounds that keep a walk of a directory with very many entries within a request's deadline and the pause budget. See IC-1, "The walk". |

The levers in "Levers" are grouped by who sets them. The deployer installs the chart and owns the nodes. The operator defines pools and grants them to tenants. The runtime author writes the Runtime definition and the runtime. The tenant chooses among the runtimes and pools it has access to and can derive its own runtimes.

### Terms from other designs and the platform

This design builds on mechanisms the specification, the platform, and other designs define. The table gives each one the meaning this document relies on.

| Term | Meaning |
| :-- | :-- |
| Checkpoint | A save of a session's workspace to the object store, from which the session resumes on another pod after a pod failure, an eviction, or a drain ([§4.4 Event / Checkpoint Store](../../spec/04_system-components.md#44-event--checkpoint-store)). The gateway starts checkpoints on a periodic schedule and before a scale-down, a drain, a CheckpointBarrier, and an eviction. |
| Tar chunk and partial manifest | Under the current method, a tar chunk is one fixed-size slice of the tar stream, uploaded as its own object. A partial manifest is the Postgres row of an attempt that did not finish. It records the session, the attempt's `checkpoint_id`, the object key prefix and size of its tar chunks, and the number of chunks and bytes uploaded, so that a resume can reassemble the tar prefix ([§10.1.7 preStop Hook Drain](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1017-prestop-hook-drain), **Partial manifest on checkpoint timeout**). |
| Workspace and slot | A slot is one of the places on a pod where a session runs ([`0145-session-isolation`](../0145-session-isolation/change.md), "SI-1"). A pod has a fixed slot count, the pool's `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod`, and a pod with one slot is laid out as a pod with several. Every slot container sees the same paths on every pod ([`0118-agent-pod-layout`, "PL-3"](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)): the session's workspace is `/workspace`, the slot's workspace volume mounted at its root and the runtime's working directory, and the runtime's private per-session state is `/session`, a second disk-backed volume. A checkpoint captures both. No runtime-visible path carries a session identifier. |
| Slot container, slot UID, and slot volumes | The slot container is the runtime container that serves one slot. It runs its own runtime supervisor and runtime process under the slot UID, a UID that belongs to that slot index and is distinct from every other slot's UID and from the adapter's within the pod. Pools on runc run with Kubernetes user namespaces (`hostUsers: false`), so each pod maps its slot UIDs to a host UID range of its own ([`0136-placement`, "User namespaces on the standard class"](../0136-placement/change.md#user-namespaces-on-the-standard-class)). The slot volumes are `slot-<n>-workspace` at `/workspace`, `slot-<n>-session` at `/session`, `slot-<n>-tmp` at `/tmp`, `slot-<n>-dshm` at `/dev/shm`, and `slot-<n>-run` at `/run/lenny`. The adapter mounts the workspace, session, and run volumes under `/adapter/slots/<n>/`, and no other slot container mounts any of them. Each is mounted whole, with no `subPath` ([PL-5](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction)). |
| Runtime supervisor and `CH-SUPERVISE` | The runtime supervisor is the platform's small C program that is the first process of every runtime container, staged in `/lenny/bin` ([`0118-agent-pod-layout`, "PL-1"](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-1-pod-blueprint)). It launches the runtime process when the adapter asks and answers the adapter on `CH-SUPERVISE`, the connection between the two ([§4.7.10 Deployment Model](../../spec/04_system-components.md#4710-deployment-model), **Runtime supervisor.**; [§28.5.3](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#2853-intra-pod)). Its worker runs setup programs and the materialization helper asynchronously, so the supervisor keeps serving the broker while a helper run is in progress ([`0145-session-isolation`](../0145-session-isolation/change.md), "SI-3"). |
| File-descriptor broker and the `fs` request | The supervisor's handling, under decision D9 of `0145-session-isolation`, that opens, creates, stats, and watches entries in its slot's volumes for the adapter and returns the descriptors over `SCM_RIGHTS`. The adapter sends an `fs` request on `CH-SUPERVISE` that names one broker root, one operation, and up to 253 paths. The supervisor resolves each path beneath that root with `openat2` and `RESOLVE_BENEATH \| RESOLVE_NO_XDEV \| RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS \| RESOLVE_NO_MAGICLINKS`, acts as the slot UID under umask 0, and replies with error numbers and other integers only, so no byte the runtime wrote crosses the channel. The operations this design uses are `open`, `openpath`, `statat`, `mkfile`, `mkdir`, `mkfifo`, `symlink`, `link`, `utimens`, `chmod`, `inotify`, and `watch` (IC-1, "Reading the slot's files"). `statat` returns, for each path, the integers of the entry's identity, its type, and its link count, and passes no descriptor ([decision D16](#decisions)). |
| Broker root | A directory descriptor that the supervisor opens on each slot volume it brokers, `workspace` on `/workspace` and `session` on `/session`, when it starts and before any session binds to the slot, while no slot code runs, and beneath which it resolves every request for the pod's life. Before the pod reports READY the adapter asks for each root through the broker and records its device and mount ID, and it checks every later descriptor and every `statx` result against that recorded identity ([`0145-session-isolation`](../0145-session-isolation/change.md), "SI-5"). |
| Spool volume | `lenny-spool`, the `emptyDir` that only the adapter mounts, at `/adapter/spool`. Slot `n`'s checkpoint spool is `/adapter/spool/<n>/checkpoint/`, its export clones are under `/adapter/spool/<n>/export/`, and its upload staging is under `/adapter/spool/<n>/staging/`, counted against the staging space the slots share ([PL-3](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout), [`0153-workspace-materialization`, "WM-4"](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#wm-4-the-plan-the-adapter-builds)). |
| Sweep, slot release, and dead slot | The Sweep is the supervisor's removal of the slot UID's files and IPC objects from the slot's writable volumes. A slot release is the sequence at the end of each session in a slot: the supervisor ends every process in the slot container, the Sweep runs, the pool's cleanup commands run, the supervisor ends every process again, a second Sweep removes what the cleanup commands left, the adapter removes what remains, the emptiness check runs, and the warm-up checkouts are staged. No runtime process is launched at release. The next session's runtime is launched at its bind, after materialization and setup. A dead slot is a slot whose container ended, whose supervisor link failed, whose request missed its deadline, whose broker reply the adapter refused, whose slot release failed, including a release that left a brokered volume not empty, or whose supervisor failed its self-test, and it serves no session for the rest of the pod's life and counts in the pod's occupancy ([`0145-session-isolation`](../0145-session-isolation/change.md), "SI-3" and "SI-4"). |
| Materialization helper | `lenny-materialize`, the Lenny binary in `/lenny/bin` that the supervisor's worker runs as the slot UID. Its subcommands write a session's workspace from the plan and run every git step on a slot repository. This design uses two of them: `capture-copy`, which copies into a slot repository the objects it holds only through a warm repository store, and `restore-complete`, which reconnects a restored repository to the pod's store ([`0153-workspace-materialization`, "WM-10"](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#wm-10-git)). |
| Gateway | Lenny's API gateway. Its checkpointer schedules checkpoint attempts (`SweepDue` runs the attempts that are due), and the gateway signs every object-store grant and writes the checkpoint records in Postgres. |
| Runtime and Runtime definition | The runtime is the agent program a pod runs. The Runtime definition ([§5.1 Runtime](../../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#51-runtime)) is the platform object that describes it. A derived runtime starts from a base Runtime. |
| Pool and warm pod | A pool is a deployment target of warm pods for one runtime and one operational profile ([§5.2 Pool Configuration and Execution Modes](../../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#52-pool-configuration-and-execution-modes)). A warm pod is a pod the pool starts before any session needs it ([§6 Warm Pod Model](../../spec/06_warm-pod-model.md#6-warm-pod-model)). The pool's configuration holds most of the settings this design adds. |
| Resource class | A pool dimension, `small`, `medium`, or `large`, that sets the resource requests of the pool's pods, including the `ephemeral-storage` request (§5.2). |
| Pod controller | The WarmPoolController, which creates a pool's warm pods and renders their pod specs ([§4.6.1 Warm Pool Controller (Pod Lifecycle)](../../spec/04_system-components.md#461-warm-pool-controller-pod-lifecycle)). The rendering code is in `pkg/controller/agentpod/podspec`. |
| Claim and bind | A claim reserves a warm pod for a session, and bind attaches the session to that pod and prepares its workspace. A derived session restores its starting snapshot at its first bind. |
| Adapter | `lenny-adapter`, the container in each agent pod that bridges the gateway and the runtime ([§4.7 Runtime Adapter](../../spec/04_system-components.md#47-runtime-adapter)). It runs the checkpoint inside the pod, and it reaches a slot's files only through that slot's file-descriptor broker. Each session's runtime runs in its slot container beside it. |
| Runtime capabilities and the handshake | A runtime declares the capabilities it implements in the handshake that opens its connection to the adapter, in place of the integration levels Basic, Standard, and Full that [§15.4.3](../../spec/15_external-api-surface.md#1543-runtime-integration-levels) states today ([`0139-runtime-protocol`, "RP-1" and "RP-2"](../0139-runtime-protocol/change.md#rp-2-capabilities-and-their-dependencies), its decision D3). The `checkpoint` capability means the runtime takes part in the quiesce handshake. |
| `CH-MSGSOCK` and `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` | `CH-MSGSOCK` is the intra-pod runtime connection on which the adapter and a runtime exchange JSON Lines frames ([§28.5.3 Intra-pod](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#2853-intra-pod)). Today the quiesce handshake and `interrupt_request` travel on a second socket, `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`. Under decision O7 (a) of [`0139-runtime-protocol`](../0139-runtime-protocol/change.md#open-decisions), which the owner settled on 2026-11-12, `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` leaves and its frames travel on `CH-MSGSOCK`, sent only when the capability that needs them is effective for the session. That changes no rule of this design. |
| Quiesce handshake | The adapter sends `checkpoint_request` with `deadlineMs`, the runtime stops writing and answers `checkpoint_ready`, and the adapter sends `checkpoint_complete` when the runtime may continue. A runtime resumes on its own 60 seconds after `checkpoint_ready`. |
| Interrupt | A request to stop a session's current work. The adapter sends `interrupt_request` with `deadlineMs` on `CH-MSGSOCK`, and the runtime answers `interrupt_acknowledged` once it reaches a safe stop point. If no acknowledgement arrives within `deadlineMs`, the adapter reports `INTERRUPT_TIMEOUT` to the gateway. The specification has the adapter move the session to `suspended` anyway ([§28.5.3](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#2853-intra-pod)), and [`0146-session-lifecycle`, "SL-3"](../0146-session-lifecycle/change.md#sl-3-suspension-and-the-open-turn) replaces that with a timeout that writes no session state. The adapter's operation lock keeps an interrupt and a checkpoint from running at the same time. |
| `Checkpoint` stream | The gRPC stream in `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto` on which the gateway drives a checkpoint in the adapter and grants each upload. [§28](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#28-communication-channels) registers it as the channel `CH-CHECKPOINT`. |
| Operation lock | The adapter's pod-wide lock (`s.ops.Begin` in `pkg/adapter/oplock.go`) that serializes a checkpoint with an interrupt and other pod operations. |
| Object-store grant | The pod holds no object-store credential. For each object the pod may write or read, the gateway signs a presigned URL that names one method and one key and expires after `checkpointCapabilityTTLSeconds`, 30 seconds by default ([§13.2.3 Checkpoint Capability Model](../../spec/13_security-model.md#1323-checkpoint-capability-model)). This design calls such a URL a grant, and §13.2.3 calls it a capability. The gateway's `checkpointGrantWindow`, 4 by default, caps the upload grants outstanding at once ([§10.1](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#101-horizontal-scaling)), and a pool can override it with `PodSet.checkpointGrantWindow`, which this design caps at 8. Under this design it counts a session's grants across its `Checkpoint` and `ExportOutputFile` streams, on the session's coordinating replica (IC-3). |
| Output file | A file the agent produces for the user, which a `file` output part names by its workspace path. The adapter copies it to the artifact store on the `ExportOutputFile` stream with this design's chunks, packs, and grants, and the gateway offers it as a download ([`0135-message-delivery`, "MD-18 Files"](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#md-18-files)). An `output_files` row records it. |
| Export and `exportTimeoutSeconds` | An export is the copy of one output file over the `ExportOutputFile` stream. `exportTimeoutSeconds` is the pool setting, 900 seconds by default, after which the gateway fails an export that has not reached `ExportReady`. |
| `CH-OUTPUTFILE` | The channel that carries the `ExportOutputFile` stream on the gateway's gRPC link to the adapter, beside `CH-CHECKPOINT`, which [`0135-message-delivery`, "MD-18 Files"](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#md-18-files) names under the specification's naming law. |
| Coordinating replica | The gateway replica that holds a session's coordination lease and drives its streams to the pod. A coordinator handoff moves the lease to another replica ([§10.1](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#101-horizontal-scaling)). |
| Coordinator fence | Step 2 of the coordinator handoff protocol of [§10.1.2](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1012-coordinator-handoff-protocol): the new coordinator sends `CoordinatorFence` with its generation, and the pod accepts the previous coordinator's RPCs until it acknowledges the fence and refuses them afterwards. |
| Quota reservation and `Rehydrate` | Under [§11.2 Budgets and Quotas](../../spec/11_policy-and-controls.md#112-budgets-and-quotas), a reservation is an atomic increment of the tenant's storage counter in Redis ahead of a write, released once by a guarded update of `reservation_released_at` (**Reservation release is exactly-once and guarded.**). `Rehydrate` rebuilds the counter from Postgres and adds every reservation not yet released (**The relative decrement is rebuild-safe.**). |
| Tombstone retention | A soft-deleted catalog row stays in Postgres for `gc.tombstoneRetentionSeconds`, 24 hours by default, before the hard-prune sweep of [§12.5](../../spec/12_storage-architecture.md#125-artifact-store) removes it. |
| Isolation profile | A pool's `isolationProfile`, which selects a Kubernetes RuntimeClass ([§5.3 Isolation Profiles](../../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#53-isolation-profiles)): `standard` runs on runc, `sandboxed` on gVisor, and `microvm` on Kata Containers. `sandboxed` is the default. |
| runc | The default container runtime, which runs containers on the node's kernel and isolates them with namespaces and cgroups. |
| gVisor | A container runtime (`runsc`) whose user-space kernel, the sentry, handles the sandbox's system calls. By default each container reaches a volume through its own gofer mount, which appears as `9p` in the pod. Systrap and KVM are the platforms gVisor can use to intercept system calls, and directfs lets the sentry open host files directly. |
| Kata Containers | A container runtime that runs each pod in a lightweight virtual machine with its own guest kernel. By default the pod reaches its volumes through the shared file system virtio-fs, and `emptydir_mode = "block-plain"` in Kata's `configuration.toml` places each `emptyDir` on a block device instead. |
| `emptyDir` and `sizeLimit` | A Kubernetes volume backed by a directory on the node for the pod's lifetime. The kubelet evicts the pod when the volume's usage exceeds its `sizeLimit`. |
| inotify | The Linux interface that reports file system events, such as a create or a write, on watched directories. Each watch counts against per-user kernel limits, and a full event queue reports `IN_Q_OVERFLOW`. |
| Resume | Restoring a session onto a new pod from its latest checkpoint ([§7.3 Retry and Resume](../../spec/07_session-lifecycle.md#73-retry-and-resume)). |
| Derive and plan | A derive starts a new session from an existing one. A derive request with `from: latest` starts from the source's snapshot, and one with `from: start` rebuilds the workspace from the source's plan ([`0144-session-derive`, "What each mode starts from"](../0144-session-derive/change.md#what-each-mode-starts-from)). The plan is the session's `WorkspacePlan`, the declarative description of how its workspace is prepared, such as the files to upload and the git repositories to clone ([§14 Workspace Plan Schema](../../spec/14_workspace-plan-schema.md#14-workspace-plan-schema)). A `gitClone` source in a plan clones a git repository over HTTPS at a branch, tag, or commit. |
| Minimal-state record | The Postgres record from which a session resumes its conversation without its workspace when no checkpoint can be used, with `resumeMode: conversation_only` and `workspaceLost: true` ([§4.4.5](../../spec/04_system-components.md#445-checkpoint-storage-failure-and-eviction-fallback)). |
| preStop drain and tiered drain cap | Before a pod ends, its preStop hook drains it ([§10.1.7 preStop Hook Drain](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1017-prestop-hook-drain)). An agent pod's hook takes an eviction checkpoint of its sessions, and a gateway pod's hook waits for the checkpoints in progress of the sessions it coordinates. Each wait is capped by a tier table keyed on the session's last measured workspace size: 30 seconds up to 100 MB, 60 seconds up to 300 MB, and 90 seconds up to the 512 MB limit. IC-7, "Drain caps", replaces the table with one cap, because the tiers stop at today's 512 MiB limit and decision D3 raises the default limit to 10 GiB. |
| CheckpointBarrier | During a rolling update of the gateway, the CheckpointBarrier protocol holds tool-call dispatch while sessions checkpoint, and the gateway waits up to `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` for each pod's `CheckpointBarrierAck` ([§10.1.8](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1018-rolling-updates-and-the-checkpointbarrier-protocol)). |
| Pool-config admission webhook and its floors | `lenny-pool-config-validator`, the admission webhook that checks every pool configuration write ([§10.1.7](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1017-prestop-hook-drain)). Its agent-pod grace floor rejects a pool whose `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` is below `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod × evictionCheckpointCapSeconds + 30`, where 30 seconds is the stream-drain budget. Its BarrierAck floor rejects a pool whose `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` is below `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod × evictionCheckpointCapSeconds`. `evictionCheckpointCapSeconds` is the single drain cap of IC-7, "Drain caps", in place of the specification's `max_tiered_checkpoint_cap`, the largest tier the pool's `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` allows, and IC-7 scales the BarrierAck floor with the slot count, which the specification's floor does not. |
| Legal hold and erasure | A legal hold keeps a session's stored data from deletion and rotation. `DeleteByUser` erases a user's data ([§12.8 Compliance Interfaces](../../spec/12_storage-architecture.md#128-compliance-interfaces)). |
| Security hold, escrow, and `EnsureCopied` | A security hold is the platform-only hold of [`0128-forensics-evidence-preservation`, "FE-3"](../0128-forensics-evidence-preservation/change.md#fe-3-platform-only-holds-and-a-security-retention-class), which the tenant under investigation can neither see nor release. It copies held checkpoints to escrow, the per-region bucket that §12.8 uses for legal-hold copies. `EnsureCopied` is FE-3's call that every path deleting held material makes first, so that the hold's copy exists before the original goes. |
| Removal of the embedded deployment model | [§4.7.10 Deployment Model](../../spec/04_system-components.md#4710-deployment-model) states today that a first-party binary may embed the adapter, so that adapter and agent are one process in one container. [`0118-agent-pod-layout`, "PL-2"](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-2-removal-of-the-embedded-deployment-model) removes that model: every agent pod has the adapter container, `netagent`, and one slot container per slot. |
| Embedded `SIGSTOP` checkpoint path | The path of [§4.4.3](../../spec/04_system-components.md#443-checkpoint-quiescence-timeout-and-abort-handling) **Embedded adapter mode only:** under which an embedded adapter stops the agent with `SIGSTOP` for a checkpoint and resumes it with `SIGCONT`. Its watchdog sets the `checkpointStuck` flag, and **Embedded adapter liveness integration:** fails the `/healthz` liveness endpoint while the flag is set. IC-9 removes all of it. |
| Revision, versioned field, and live field | A revision is an immutable, numbered copy of a Runtime's versioned fields. A change to a versioned field creates a revision, and a session reads those fields from the revision chosen for it. A change to a live field applies to the next session on every revision and creates no revision ([`0140-runtime-revisions`, "RV-1 Revisions"](../0140-runtime-revisions/change.md#rv-1-revisions)). |
| Storage reservation | The `Reserve` charge that [`0144-session-derive`](../0144-session-derive/change.md#what-it-refuses) takes on the tenant's storage counter ahead of a derive's first copy, which `undoDerivedWrites` releases when a later step fails. |
| Session envelope | The session object the API returns to a client. |
| `.lennyignore` | A file in a workspace that lists paths to leave out, in the format of `.gitignore`. The CLI honors it when it uploads a workspace. |
| `final_workspace_ref` | The reference to a session's final workspace snapshot, recorded at session end. |
| F-n.n.n | A finding filed in `BUILD-GAPS.md` at the repository root. |

### Benchmark terms

"Benchmarks" names its scenarios and configurations with these terms. Its "Setup" states them in full.

| Term | Meaning |
| :-- | :-- |
| Steady | A checkpoint after 50 files changed. |
| After checkout | A checkpoint after `git checkout HEAD~2000`, on the repositories with full history. |
| Package install | A checkpoint that starts 3 seconds after a writer begins creating 150,000 files of 4 KiB at 10,000 files per second. |
| First checkpoint | A session's first checkpoint, whose pre-copy reads and uploads the whole workspace. |
| Configuration | One row of a benchmark table: an isolation profile and the variant of it measured. "gVisor" is the systrap platform with directfs. "gVisor exclusive" adds `--file-access-mounts=exclusive`, and "gVisor KVM" adds `--platform=kvm`. "Pod-share mount hint" is the gVisor annotations that make the workspace one mount shared by the pod's containers (IC-1, "Change tracker"). "Kata" is the default virtio-fs cache mode, `auto`, and "Kata metadata" and "Kata always" set `virtio_fs_cache` to those values. "Kata block" places the workspace on a block device, as `emptydir_mode = "block-plain"` does. |
| Metal, 2 CPUs | "Metal" marks a run on the bare-metal `m7i.metal-24xl`. The other rows of the pause and restore tables ran on `m8i.2xlarge` with nested virtualization enabled, so Kata and gVisor KVM ran a virtual machine inside the EC2 virtual machine. "2 CPUs" marks a pod limited to 2 CPUs and 4 GiB instead of 4 CPUs and 8 GiB. |
| gp3 baseline | An EC2 gp3 volume at its included performance of 3,000 IOPS (input/output operations per second) and 125 MiB/s. |
| Verification walk | A walk after each capture, while the workload was still stopped, that counted the changes the capture missed. |

### Labels from other designs

This document cites the following labels from other designs.

| Label | Design | What it names |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| EG-2 and EG-3 | [`0127-egress-and-git`, "Proposal split"](../0127-egress-and-git/change.md#steps) | The egress design's proposals for the pod network agent and proxied egress (EG-2), which also renders the workspace volume's `sizeLimit`, and for the pod git path, which runs git inside the agent pod (EG-3). |
| FE-1 and FE-3 | [`0128-forensics-evidence-preservation`](../0128-forensics-evidence-preservation/change.md) | [FE-1](../0128-forensics-evidence-preservation/change.md#fe-1-quarantine) quarantine of an agent pod, and [FE-3](../0128-forensics-evidence-preservation/change.md#fe-3-platform-only-holds-and-a-security-retention-class) platform-only security holds. |
| MD-3, MD-18, and A12 to A21 | [`0135-message-delivery`](../0135-message-delivery/change.md) | [MD-3](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#md-3-inbox-states-and-custody) inbox states and the consumed message-id set a checkpoint records, [MD-18](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#md-18-files) how a file the agent produces for the user is frozen, copied to the artifact store, offered as a download, and kept for the session's retention, [A12](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#decisions) the owner's decision of 2026-11-10 that such a file is copied with this design's chunks, packs, and grants and shares the session's grant window with its checkpoints, A13 to A17, the owner's decisions of the same day on the per-pool grant window and its maximum of 8, the restated S5 bound, the export lifecycle, holds over output files, and the pause of export work during a capture pass, and A18 to A21, the owner's decisions of the same day on the pack nonce, the seal window that the seal checkpoint and the exports share, the whole-file SHA-256 as published metadata, and the files a reflink pool does not clone. |
| SI-1 to SI-6, SI decisions D8 and D9, SI O2, O8, and O11 | [`0145-session-isolation`](../0145-session-isolation/change.md) | SI-1 the slot containers and slot volumes, which [`0118-agent-pod-layout`](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md) now lays out, SI-2 to SI-4 slot identity, slot release, and dead slots, [SI-5](../0145-session-isolation/change.md#si-5-the-supervisor-file-descriptor-broker) the file-descriptor broker and its operations, [SI-6](../0145-session-isolation/change.md#si-6-checkpoint-restore-export-and-cleanup) the adapter's access to a slot's files, the owner's decisions D8 (whole-volume slot mounts and an adapter-only spool volume) and D9 (the file-descriptor broker) of 2026-11-12, O2 the report of a file the adapter cannot read, O8 the user namespaces that runc pools require, and O11 the git steps on a slot repository, which run as the slot UID. SI's D8 and D9 are distinct from this design's D8 and D9, and this document always names them with the prefix "SI". |
| PL-1 to PL-5 and PL decisions D1 to D3 | [`0118-agent-pod-layout`](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md) | [PL-1](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-1-pod-blueprint) the containers of an agent pod, [PL-2](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-2-removal-of-the-embedded-deployment-model) the removal of the embedded deployment model, [PL-3](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout) the runtime-visible paths `/workspace` and `/session` and the adapter's paths under `/adapter`, and [PL-5](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction) the volumes with their size limits and the per-slot disk watch. |
| WM-4, WM-10, WM-11, WM-13, WM-15, and WM O9 | [`0153-workspace-materialization`](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md) | [WM-4](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#wm-4-the-plan-the-adapter-builds) upload staging, one source at a time, in the staging space the slots share, [WM-10](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#wm-10-git) the git steps of the materialization helper, among them `capture-copy` and `restore-complete`, [WM-11](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#wm-11-the-worker-broker-requests-and-checkpoints) the supervisor's worker and how broker requests and checkpoints proceed during a helper run, [WM-13](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#wm-13-resume-and-derive) resume and derive, [WM-15](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#wm-15-warm-up-checkout-and-reflink-rebuild) the warm-up checkout and the `lenny-store-fill` container that fills the stores and their pristine trees, and O9, which the owner settled on 2026-11-13: the pod-wide `spoolSizeLimitBytes` and how the adapter divides it. |
| EG decisions 59 and 61 | [`0127-egress-and-git`, "Decisions"](../0127-egress-and-git/change.md#decisions) | Decision 59 puts `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false` in the rendered git configuration, which adopts this design's D6. Decision 61, which the owner settled on 2026-11-13, moves the warm store fill out of the adapter into the `lenny-store-fill` container. |
| AR | [`0119-agent-runtimes-only`](../0119-agent-runtimes-only/change.md) | The removal of `type: mcp` runtimes and service mode, after which every pod is an agent pod with slots that serves one session per slot. |
| RP-1, RP-2, RP D3, and RP O7 | [`0139-runtime-protocol`](../0139-runtime-protocol/change.md) | RP-1 the handshake, [RP-2](../0139-runtime-protocol/change.md#rp-2-capabilities-and-their-dependencies) the capabilities a runtime declares, among them `checkpoint`, D3 the owner's decision that capabilities replace the integration levels, and O7, settled on 2026-11-12 as (a), under which `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` merges into `CH-MSGSOCK`. |
| PM-1 and PM-2 | [`0136-placement`](../0136-placement/change.md) | [PM-1](../0136-placement/change.md#pm-1-node-classes-and-presets) node classes, the separation of runc nodes, and user namespaces on the standard class, and [PM-2](../0136-placement/change.md#pm-2-node-tenancy) node tenancy, under which a pool's pods run on nodes dedicated to one tenant. |
| RV-1 and RV-2 | [`0140-runtime-revisions`](../0140-runtime-revisions/change.md) | [RV-1](../0140-runtime-revisions/change.md#rv-1-revisions) the revision record and its field classes, and [RV-2](../0140-runtime-revisions/change.md#rv-2-selection) the choice of a revision for each session. |
## High-level design

When a session's pod fails or is drained, Lenny resumes the session on another pod from its last checkpoint, a saved copy of the session's files in object storage. Today each checkpoint packs the whole workspace into one archive and uploads it, and an agent that cooperates with checkpoints stays paused until the upload ends. A workspace holding a large repository therefore pauses the agent for minutes, fails the 60-second checkpoint timeout every time, and uploads every byte again on each attempt.

This design uploads only what changed, and it pauses the agent only while it collects the last few changes. While the agent runs, Lenny copies the files that changed since the last checkpoint. It then pauses the agent, takes the files that changed during that copy, and resumes the agent before uploading them. Files are stored as pieces named by a hash of their content, so content already stored is not uploaded again and a failed attempt's uploads are reused. For a checkout of the Chromium repository, 516,000 files in 5.9 GiB, the measured pause fell from 225 to 625 seconds to between 1.1 and 8.6 seconds when Lenny finds changes by scanning the files, and to between 0.11 and 0.16 seconds when a watcher records changes as they happen. The limits are that, without the watcher, the pause grows with the number of files in the workspace, that the watcher runs under gVisor only with particular node configuration, and that a restore still downloads the whole workspace.

Each session runs in its own slot container under its own UID, as [`0145-session-isolation`](../0145-session-isolation/change.md) and [`0118-agent-pod-layout`](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md) lay out the pod. The adapter, which runs the checkpoint, reaches the session's files only through descriptors that the slot's runtime supervisor opens for it, and it keeps all checkpoint logic itself. Through those descriptors it reads every file the session's owner can open, and a restore puts back each file's owner, mode, time stamps, and hard links exactly. A re-run on AWS through those descriptors measured Chromium's scanning pause at 1.29 s under runc, 1.51 s on a Kata block volume, 2.55 s and 6.33 s on gVisor's two mounts, and 9.44 s on Kata's shared file system, the watcher's pause at 0.013 to 0.094 s, and restores within 1.0 to 1.2 times the time of writing the files directly. The slots of one pod checkpoint one at a time, because concurrent scans in one gVisor sandbox slowed each other by 1.4 to 1.8 times.

The rest of this section states each part in the design's terms, which "Terms" above defines.

```
            pre-copy pass               capture pass          upload pass
runtime:    running ------------------- | quiesced | ------------ running ------->
adapter:    read files whose identity     take the changed      pack spooled chunks,
            changed, upload new packs     set (tracker or       upload packs and
                                          walk), read or        manifest
                                          clone into spool
gateway:    grants a PUT per pack ............................. checks reference closure,
                                                                writes the snapshot row
```

**Capture in passes (IC-1).** A checkpoint runs a pre-copy pass, a capture pass, and an upload pass. Pre-copy reads every file that has no trusted entry in the file index and uploads its new chunks while the runtime runs. A quiescing runtime is quiesced only during the capture pass, in which the adapter takes the changed set from the change tracker, or from a stat walk when no healthy tracker runs, and reads or clones those files into the spool. The upload pass packs and uploads the spool after the runtime resumes. Before it asks the runtime to pause, the adapter estimates the capture time from the slowest rates it has observed. When 1.5 times the estimate exceeds the pause budget, the adapter runs further pre-copy rounds and then captures on the best-effort path. A runtime that does not quiesce is always captured best-effort, and a file that changes during that capture is flagged `torn` without failing the attempt. Git lock files and temporary git packfiles are left out, and git metadata is captured before git objects. Every pass reaches the slot's files through the file-descriptor broker of the slot's runtime supervisor, and the stat walk lists directories through broker-passed descriptors and reads identities with the adapter's own `statx` calls relative to them. The spool is on a volume that only the adapter mounts. A pod's slots checkpoint one at a time.

**Storage format (IC-2).** Files are cut into content-defined chunks named by their SHA-256, compressed with zstd, and stored in packs of about 16 MiB under the writing session's prefix. Each pack carries a random nonce, so no two packs share an ID. A manifest lists every path with its chunk IDs and the metadata an exact restore needs, link groups and sparse extents included, and Postgres holds the snapshot root.

**Checkpoint stream (IC-3).** The gateway still drives each checkpoint over the `Checkpoint` stream and grants each upload, now one pack at a time. `checkpoint_complete` now means the snapshot is captured, and the gateway runs due attempts concurrently. An `ExportOutputFile` stream beside it copies an output file of [MD-18](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#md-18-files) on the same packs and grants.

**Catalog, reference closure, and quota (IC-4).** Postgres records packs, snapshots, output files, and which snapshot or output file references which pack. The gateway accepts a snapshot only when every pack it references is in the session's allowed set, so a pod cannot read another session's content by naming a hash. The allowed set also holds the platform chunk sets of the sources the session may read: the working trees of a warm repository store's warm-up checkouts and a runtime's shared assets, which only pods that have never run a session add to, so a session's first checkpoint does not upload a large repository's unchanged files. The tenant is charged once for each pack.

**Restore, derive, download, and seal (IC-5).** A restore fetches the snapshot's packs, verifies every chunk, creates every entry of `/workspace` and `/session` through the file-descriptor broker as the slot UID with its recorded mode and time stamps, and builds the file index from the files it wrote. The materialization helper's `restore-complete` then reconnects restored repositories to the pod's warm repository stores. A derive references the source's packs and copies no object. The workspace download route still returns a gzip-compressed tar, which the gateway renders. The seal runs the same passes at session end, and the session's exports still running finish in the time the seal leaves in the seal window.

**Retention and garbage collection (IC-6).** The latest 2 snapshots per session are retained, and output files follow the session's retention. A pack is deleted when no live snapshot or output file references it and its lease has expired, sparse packs are compacted, and erasure and legal hold apply to snapshots and output files.

**Size limits and drain budgets (IC-7).** `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` bounds disk use and storage instead of the pause, and its default rises from 512 MiB to 10 GiB. The pod controller renders every disk-backed volume's `sizeLimit` and an `ephemeral-storage` request equal to their sum plus the resource class's own request (D21). The spool is one pod-wide volume that the slots share. An attempt gets its own timeout, and the tiered drain caps become one cap.

**Checkpoint policy, interval, and change tracker (IC-8).** A Runtime and a pool can each set `checkpointPolicy` to `full`, `sealOnly`, or `none`, and the stricter value applies. A session under `sealOnly` or `none` never becomes dormant: it stays idle on its pod, and the idle timeout ends it. Each can set its own checkpoint interval, with the pool's value winning, within the deployer's bounds. The pool's `checkpointChangeTracker` can turn the tracker off. Under its default, `auto`, the adapter runs the tracker only on slot file systems where the supervisor-held tracker is known to see every change, which the re-run confirmed for every file system it measured, and leaves it off on gVisor's default mount, where Chromium's watches exhaust the sandbox's descriptors.

**Removal of the embedded `SIGSTOP` path (IC-9).** The specification lets an embedded adapter stop the agent with `SIGSTOP` for a checkpoint. IC-9 removes that path, its `checkpointStuck` flag, and the liveness rule built on the flag. The embedded deployment model itself leaves under [PL-2](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-2-removal-of-the-embedded-deployment-model), so every runtime is checkpointed by the same cooperative or best-effort rule through its slot's broker.

**Levers.** "Levers" lists the settings with which deployers, operators, runtime authors, and tenants lower the pause or how often it occurs, with the measured effect and the cost of each.

## Owner's direction

On 2026-11-05 the owner wrote: "we definitely need to improve checkpointing with a re-design." The owner supplied pointers from another conversation, which "Starting points" evaluates.

On the same day the owner accepted the recommended `checkpointPolicy` values (IC-8, [decision D8](#decisions)), in the words "your recommendation makes sense", and added: "I think a runtime/pool should be able to override the default checkpoint interval if possible."

The owner then asked for measurements on AWS, run independently of the project's test infrastructure, with repositories fetched from the internet, and scoped to "how long it takes to checkpoint and transfer". For the follow-up runs the owner wrote: "Depending on the results, and if needed to find a good solution, try different combinations. If 4 helps, then it should be documented for deployers. Make sure to measure performance impacts of 5." Item 4 is the gVisor KVM platform, and item 5 is the inotify change tracker. The owner added: "Once we're done with the tests and decisions, make sure the design doc includes changes/updates to Lenny's docs, with maybe a new section for checkpointing, with actual benchmarks and all the levers operators and tenants can use to reduce the impact when needed (including things like disabling checkpointing or changing the frequency, running on runc in a dedicated pool (tenant pool), etc.)"

Asked whether the default pause budget should be 5 s or 10 s, the owner chose "10 s default" ([decision D7](#decisions)). The same day the owner asked for the Kubernetes checks in "Benchmarks": "Test --file-access-mounts=exclusive with ConfigMaps etc. Test emptydir_mode = "block-plain"'s interaction with the kubelet's emptyDir size limit."

Asked whether the platform has a way to stop a runtime that does not quiesce, such as the cgroup freezer, which suspends every process in a container's control group, the owner answered: "no, I don't want the forcing mechanism, thanks". Quiescence therefore stays cooperative. A quiescing runtime pauses itself on `checkpoint_request`, and every other runtime is captured on the best-effort path while it runs.

On 2026-11-08 the owner accepted, in the word "ok", the recommended placement of the settings this design names with defaults ([decision D14](#decisions)). The pack format and capture settings are constants that the specification states, `checkpointConcurrency` is a gateway setting from the chart, and `restoreConcurrency` is an adapter setting that the controller passes from the chart to every agent pod.

On 2026-11-09 the owner settled that the embedded `SIGSTOP` checkpoint path of §4.4.3 is removed, and that this design states the removal ([decision D15](#decisions), IC-9). [`0128-forensics-evidence-preservation`](../0128-forensics-evidence-preservation/change.md) had proposed to amend that path for quarantined pods, and it drops the amendment.

On 2026-11-12, after three spikes that checked this design against [`0145-session-isolation`](../0145-session-isolation/change.md), the owner settled that design's decisions D8 and D9. Under SI decision D8 each slot container mounts its whole workspace volume with no `subPath`, a separate volume that only the adapter mounts holds the spool and the export staging, and the warm clones are mounted read-only at the same absolute path in the adapter and in every slot. Under SI decision D9 the adapter reaches every slot file through the runtime supervisor's file-descriptor broker, the `fs` request on `CH-SUPERVISE`, the owner accepted about 470 added lines in the C supervisor, and all checkpoint logic stays in the adapter. The owner asked that this design and the other designs that handle session files be updated, and that the checkpoint design then get a full validation with performance measurements like the AWS benchmarks of 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-06. Revision 13 applies the update, and "Benchmarks", "Validation owed" states the validation.

The validation ran on AWS on 2026-11-12, and a second run the same day measured Kata `block-plain` with a dedicated `fsGroup` and the parallel stat walk ("Re-validation through the broker"). On those numbers the owner settled the open decisions of revision 13 the same day: "D16: adopt `statat`", "D17: keep the tracker off on 9p under auto", "D18: copy-mode freeze for files within the export share", and "The tracker can be enabled on Kata virtio-fs" ([decisions D16 to D18](#decisions)). The owner directed three fixes without a decision: FIFOs on gVisor's default mount are recorded as metadata-only, the spool's `sizeLimit` sits above the adapter's cap and any single clone is bounded by the slot's remaining share, falling back to reading in place, and the 4 GiB constant-write clone that took 11 s against 2 to 2.5 s before is recorded as an investigation owed.

The owner then asked for a faster broker walk, "with a specific concern about directories holding very large numbers of files and about timeouts", and settled it as option (b): the adapter stats in parallel relative to broker-passed directory descriptors, falls back to the broker's `statat` only on `EACCES`, and the supervisor forks no broker workers ([decision D19](#decisions)). The owner also decided to "stagger checkpoints across slots: at most one slot checkpointing at a time per pod, the others queued", as a decision of this design, with a measurement later ([decision D20](#decisions)).

On 2026-11-13 the owner asked for cross-session deduplication as an optimization, possibly as an opt-in per tenant. A study found that sharing chunks between sessions lets a session probe what other sessions hold, through the pod's network byte counters on every isolation profile, checkpoint timing, or the quota. The owner chose a hybrid: only files the platform staged, from warm repository stores and from a runtime's shared assets, are deduplicated, with one set per source and no key by commit ([decision D1](#decisions), IC-4, "Platform chunk sets").

The same day the owner settled the remaining recommendations of this design after each was explained. The owner answered "D2 ok", "D4 ok", and "D5 ok". For D3 the owner accepted the 10 GiB default of `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` and, after asking what the change to `ephemeral-storage` involves, accepted that the controller renders each disk-backed volume's `sizeLimit` and an `ephemeral-storage` request equal to their sum, and that the ResourceQuota defaults of [§17.8.2](../../spec/17_deployment-topology.md#1782-capacity-tier-reference) and the tiered drain caps of [§10.1.7](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1017-prestop-hook-drain) are recomputed. For D6 the owner asked whether a runtime can override the maintenance settings and accepted that it can, at the cost of its own session's next checkpoint. For D9 the owner chose that the pool's value wins, clamped to the deployer's bounds. For D10 the owner confirmed the tracker as designed, with `auto` as the default. For D11 the owner asked to "be a bit conservative" and accepted the slowest observed rates with a margin of 1.5: the adapter pauses only if 1.5 times the estimate fits the budget. For D13 the owner answered "yes" and added: "we must document all the relevant details of this design (and all others)". The owner also accepted the defaults this design chose in revision 14, which are the spool's headroom factor of 2, 2 fallback requests, 16 walker threads, a stagger that covers the whole attempt, and restores that are not queued. The owner required a test on pods with several runtimes before the design is final ("Validation owed", item 16).

The same pass applies owner decisions that other designs own. The embedded deployment model is removed, with the in-process broker of revision 13 ([PL-2](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-2-removal-of-the-embedded-deployment-model)). The runtime sees `/workspace` and a disk-backed `/session` on every pod ([PL-3](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)). Every git step on a slot repository, the capture copy and restore completion included, runs as the slot UID in the materialization helper ([WM-10](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#wm-10-git)), and the supervisor's worker runs asynchronously, so a helper run no longer blocks the broker. Runtimes declare capabilities in place of integration levels ([`0139-runtime-protocol`](../0139-runtime-protocol/change.md#decisions), D3). Pre-launched runtimes are removed, so every runtime launches at bind and a slot release launches nothing. runc pools require user namespaces ([PM-1](../0136-placement/change.md#pm-1-node-classes-and-presets)).

Revision 16 applies the owner's decisions of 2026-11-13 that other designs own. The spool volume is sized by one pod-wide `spoolSizeLimitBytes`, of which the adapter uses half and reserves each slot's spool cap ([`0153-workspace-materialization`](../0153-workspace-materialization/change.md#open-decisions), O9 as revised). The `lenny-store-fill` init container fills the warm repository stores and their pristine trees, so the adapter runs no git ([`0127-egress-and-git`](../0127-egress-and-git/change.md#decisions), decision 61). An idle session whose checkpoint policy is `sealOnly` or `none` stays idle on its pod and ends at the idle timeout ([`0146-session-lifecycle`, "SL-6"](../0146-session-lifecycle/change.md#sl-6-idle-pods-and-dormancy)). `type: mcp` runtimes and service mode are removed ([`0119-agent-runtimes-only`](../0119-agent-runtimes-only/change.md)), so every pod this design checkpoints is an agent pod with slots.

## Requirements

- R1. The time a runtime spends quiesced depends on the number of files in the workspace and on the bytes changed since the last pre-copy pass. It does not depend on the total bytes in the workspace. The target is a P95 pause under 1 second for a workspace of 500,000 files with up to 256 MiB changed since pre-copy, on runc with node-local SSD. Through the broker the target holds only with the change tracker: the walk alone paused Chromium's 565,000 entries for 1.29 s under runc, and the tracker's pause was 0.035 s ("Re-validation through the broker").
- R2. A checkpoint uploads only content that the session's retained snapshots do not already hold.
- R3. Bytes that a failed or timed-out attempt uploaded are reused by the next attempt of the same session.
- R4. Node disk and the tenant's storage quota bound workspace size. The checkpoint pause no longer sets that bound.
- R5. The pod keeps the capability model of [§13.2.3 Checkpoint Capability Model](../../spec/13_security-model.md#1323-checkpoint-capability-model). It holds no object-store credential and no `LIST`, `DELETE`, or multipart capability, and the gateway chooses every key it signs.
- R6. A pod can reference only content that its own session wrote or that its session started from. Naming a hash grants no read.
- R7. The design works on runc, gVisor, and Kata without node configuration, added privileges, or a particular node filesystem. A filesystem feature speeds up capture where the adapter detects it, and node configuration that a deployer applies lowers the pause further (see "Levers"). The file-descriptor broker needs no capability, and the pod passes the Pod Security Standards' Restricted profile.
- R11. A snapshot tagged `consistency: consistent` contains every change made before the runtime acknowledged the quiesce. A change source that can lose a change without reporting the loss is not used for a consistent capture.
- R8. A file that changes during a best-effort capture costs that file's consistency. The attempt still completes.
- R9. A restore verifies every byte it writes against the content hash recorded for it.
- R10. Client contracts are unchanged: `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/workspace` still returns a gzip-compressed tar, and a derive still starts from the source's snapshot.
- R12. The adapter reaches every slot file through the slot's file-descriptor broker, on every pod, every isolation profile, and every slot count, with one implementation, and all checkpoint logic stays in the adapter (SI decision D9). No path of this design reads or writes a slot file through the adapter's own mount.
- R13. A restore reproduces, for every entry of `/workspace` and `/session` the capture read, its type, its mode, its owner as the restoring slot's UID, its `mtime` to the nanosecond, its link group, its sparse extents where the capture saw them, and a symbolic link's target. The entries the capture could only record as metadata are reported to the session (IC-1, "Reading the slot's files").
- R14. At most one slot of a pod runs a checkpoint attempt at a time, and the attempts of the pod's other slots wait in a queue (IC-1, "Staggering across slots").

## Security properties

- S1. The gateway signs a `PUT` only for a pack key under the writing session's own prefix, and never for a pack key `(tenant_id, owner_session_id, pack_id)` its catalog already holds, in any state, a soft-deleted row included, so a pod cannot overwrite a pack a snapshot or an output file references or one whose deletion is still pending. The pack nonce of IC-2 makes every pack an adapter builds distinct, so an honest adapter never declares a key the catalog holds except to resend a pack it built. A `PackReady` resolves the pack ID against the session's own key first. A pack under that key that is in the session's allowed set is answered "already stored" and receives no grant, and a key held in any other state is refused (IC-3). Only when the session holds no such key does the gateway consult the allowed set's packs that another session owns, which are the packs of a derived session's starting snapshot, and only for the "already stored" answer. The check therefore reads the session's own keys and its allowed set, which the session may already read, and it reveals nothing about any other session's packs. A session's `Checkpoint` and `ExportOutputFile` streams never receive a grant for a key under a platform chunk set's prefix. The gateway signs a `PUT` for a platform pack only on `CH-SOURCECHUNKS`, only to a pod whose record shows no bind, only for a source on that pod's warm list or its runtime's shared assets, and never for a key its catalog holds (IC-4, "Platform chunk sets").
- S2. The gateway accepts a snapshot only when every pack it references is in the session's allowed set (see "Reference closure"). The allowed set comes from Postgres, and its platform packs are those of the eligible sources the gateway computes from the session's `coveredStores` and its runtime, so a pod that names a foreign pack ID, or a platform pack of a store the session is withheld from, fails the check.
- S3. The gateway signs a `GET` only for packs that the snapshot being restored references, the platform packs it references included.
- S4. A restore recomputes the SHA-256 of every chunk and fails the file whose chunk does not match, so a corrupt or substituted pack cannot silently change restored content.
- S5. A compromised pod can write up to the pool's effective `checkpointGrantWindow` times `packMaxBytes` of chosen bytes into its own session's prefix within the grant TTL. The effective window is the number of upload grants the gateway keeps outstanding at once for one session, counted across the session's `Checkpoint` and `ExportOutputFile` streams together on the session's coordinating replica: the pool's `PodSet.checkpointGrantWindow` when set, at most 8, and otherwise the gateway's default of 4 ([§10.1](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#101-horizontal-scaling)). `packMaxBytes` is the largest pack IC-2 allows, a constant of 32 MiB. The bound is therefore 128 MiB by default and at most 256 MiB, per session across both streams, and an output-file export does not add a window of its own ([A12 and A13](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#decisions)). Today's bound is `checkpointGrantWindow × partialChunkSizeBytes`, 4 × 16 MiB, so the pack format doubles it by default ([A14](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#decisions)). A new coordinator after a handoff cannot see the grants the previous one signed, and the pod accepts the previous coordinator's RPCs until it acknowledges the new coordinator's fence ([§10.1.2](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1012-coordinator-handoff-protocol), step 2). The new coordinator therefore counts the session's whole window as outstanding until `checkpointCapabilityTTLSeconds` (30 s) has passed since the pod acknowledged its fence, and the adapter cancels the session's open `Checkpoint` and `ExportOutputFile` streams of the previous generation when it acknowledges the fence, so the bound holds across the handoff. The gateway refuses at startup a `--checkpoint-grant-window` above 8, so the gateway's default window cannot exceed the bound either. The bound holds because the gateway refuses a grant for a pack whose declared length exceeds `packMaxBytes`, so the size a grant can carry is a gateway constant rather than a pod's report. The bound limits a burst of chosen bytes in the session's own prefix, which the tenant's quota pays for and garbage collection reclaims once no snapshot or output file references the pack.

- S6. The adapter treats every reply of a slot's file-descriptor broker as untrusted from the first worker run of the first session bound to the slot, or from that session's launch when no worker run precedes it.
