## Specification sections affected

- [§4.4.2 Checkpoint Atomicity and Partial Manifests](../../spec/04_system-components.md#442-checkpoint-atomicity-and-partial-manifests): the atomic unit becomes a snapshot row with its pack references, and partial manifests are removed.
- [§4.4.3 Checkpoint Quiescence, Timeout, and Abort Handling](../../spec/04_system-components.md#443-checkpoint-quiescence-timeout-and-abort-handling): the passes, the pause budget, convergence, best-effort tolerance, and the meaning of `checkpoint_complete`. The section states the capture constants `reflinkMinBytes` (1 MiB), `racyWindow` (2 s), `bestEffortRereads` (2), `walkFallbackConcurrency` (2), the tracker's event cap (16,384 events per swap), `spoolHeadroomFactor` (2), and `pauseEstimateMargin` (1.5), and the estimate from the slowest observed rates under which the adapter pauses the runtime only when 1.5 times the estimate fits the budget. It also states that every pass reaches a slot's `/workspace` and `/session` trees through the slot's file-descriptor broker with identities compared against the broker root's recorded device and mount ID, the parallel stat walk relative to broker-passed directory descriptors with its name rule, its `openat2` flags, its per-directory `statat` fallback on `EACCES`, and its large-directory rules, the metadata-only entries with the FIFO under gVisor's default mount, the tracker's inotify instance in the slot's supervisor with its barrier file created and written through the broker, its link-group rule, its event cap, and its overflow held until the next walk, the spool on `lenny-spool` with its accounting by logical size, each clone or copy bounded by the session's remaining share, and the reflink probe's modes per slot volume, the reserved helper names the walk skips, the capture copy that precedes pre-copy, and the checkpoint stagger across a pod's slots. "Full-level" and "Standard-level" become runtimes that do and do not declare `checkpoint`, as `0139-runtime-protocol` states. IC-9 removes **Embedded adapter mode only:**, the embedded adapter path of **Checkpoint timeout and recovery:**, **SIGCONT confirmation.**, the `SIGCONT` sentence of **Checkpoint abort cleanup:**, and **Embedded adapter liveness integration:**, with `checkpointStuck` and `watchdogTimeoutSeconds`.
- [§4.4.4 Checkpoint SLOs and Scheduling](../../spec/04_system-components.md#444-checkpoint-slos-and-scheduling): the SLO measures the pause, the size limit bounds disk and storage, and its default in **Hard workspace size limit.** becomes 10 GiB in place of 512 MiB (decision D3), and scheduling and freshness use each session's effective interval. The section states that the gateway runs up to `checkpointConcurrency` due attempts at once per replica. IC-9 removes the embedded adapter sentence, the `SIGSTOP` clause of **Hard workspace size limit.**, and the embedded-adapter scope of `CheckpointDurationHigh`.
- [§4.7.2 Checkpoint and Interrupt Mutual Exclusion](../../spec/04_system-components.md#472-checkpoint-and-interrupt-mutual-exclusion): **Interrupt during checkpoint:** loses its `SIGSTOP` rationale (IC-9).
- [§4.4.5 Checkpoint Storage Failure and Eviction Fallback](../../spec/04_system-components.md#445-checkpoint-storage-failure-and-eviction-fallback): retry budgets apply per pack.
- [§4.5 Artifact Store](../../spec/04_system-components.md#45-artifact-store) **Workspace lineage:**: snapshot identity and lineage.
- [§5.1 Runtime](../../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#51-runtime) and [§5.2 Pool Configuration and Execution Modes](../../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#52-pool-configuration-and-execution-modes): `checkpointPolicy` and `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` on both, and the resolution rules. §5.1 also gains `checkpointExcludes`, with a tenant's entries appended to the base revision's list, and the runtime field `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds`, which overrides the pool's seal window within `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationCeilingSeconds`, and §5.2's pool admission refuses a pool value above that ceiling ([A19](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#decisions)).
- [§4.7 Runtime Adapter](../../spec/04_system-components.md#47-runtime-adapter) and [§28](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#28-communication-channels): `FinalizeWorkspace` and `Resume` carry the session's `checkpointExcludes` list, and the adapter leaves the listed directories out of every capture of that session.
- [§4.7 Runtime Adapter](../../spec/04_system-components.md#47-runtime-adapter): the `ExportOutputFile` RPC beside `Checkpoint` with `ExportAbort`, the pack nonce in every pack footer, the freeze of a file part when the adapter reads it with the per-session cap on open export descriptors, which the adapter's count holds authoritatively with `unopened: true` on a part beyond it and the descriptor freed at the end of the export or at `output_failed`, the dedup check of each chunk when it is packed, the export share of the spool with the clones of waiting parts counted in it and a file over the remaining share read in place, the cancellation of the previous generation's open `Checkpoint` and `ExportOutputFile` streams when the adapter acknowledges a fence, the pause of export reading, hashing, and compression during a capture pass, the confirm-before-share rule of the dedup set, the eviction after `ReferenceRefused`, and the output-file chunk lists and leased packs on `Resume` and `CheckpointStart`.
- [§28.3 Registers](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#283-registers), [§28.4 Claim register](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#284-claim-register), [§28.5.1 Gateway-to-pod](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#2851-gateway-to-pod), [§28.5.5 Pod-egress](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#2855-pod-egress), and [§28.6 Exclusivity and concurrency model](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#286-exclusivity-and-concurrency-model): the `CH-OUTPUTFILE` register row and contract card with generation fencing, its claim-register row, the `CH-OBJSTORE` row amended for export packs and the per-session window, and the statement that the pod-level operation lock bounds the transfer `CH-OBJSTORE` carries amended, because an export takes no operation lock.
- [§10.1 Horizontal Scaling](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#101-horizontal-scaling), [§10.1.2 Coordinator Handoff Protocol](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1012-coordinator-handoff-protocol), and [§10.1.7 preStop Hook Drain](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1017-prestop-hook-drain): `checkpointGrantWindow` counted per session across both streams on the coordinating replica, the new coordinator's count of the whole window as outstanding until `checkpointCapabilityTTLSeconds` has passed since the pod acknowledged its fence, the adapter's cancellation of the previous generation's open streams at that acknowledgement, the per-pool override's maximum of 8 and the gateway flag's refusal above 8 at startup, the drain order in which the eviction checkpoint runs before exports with no export grant signed while it runs, and the statement that an export stream takes no part in the stream-drain stage.
- [§5.2 Pool Configuration and Execution Modes](../../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#52-pool-configuration-and-execution-modes): `checkpointChangeTracker` and `checkpointTrackerMaxWatches`, and `exportTimeoutSeconds`, counted from `ExportStart`, with the admission refusal of a pool unless `packLeaseSeconds` is at least twice the larger of its `checkpointAttemptTimeoutSeconds` and `exportTimeoutSeconds`.
- [§5.3 Isolation Profiles](../../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#53-isolation-profiles): the node configuration that lowers the pause on nodes that run pools with `isolationProfile: sandboxed` or `microvm`, stated as deployer guidance: passing `dev.gvisor.*` annotations to runsc, which the slot workspace and session volumes and `lenny-spool` use, and the effect of `--file-access-mounts=exclusive` on kubelet-updated volumes for gVisor, and the Kata guest watch limit and `emptydir_mode`.
- [§6.4 Pod Filesystem Layout](../../spec/06_warm-pod-model.md#64-pod-filesystem-layout): the pod controller renders the gVisor pod-share mount hint annotations for every slot workspace volume, every slot session volume, and `lenny-spool` on every pod of a pool with `isolationProfile: sandboxed`.
- [§6.2 Pod State Machine](../../spec/06_warm-pod-model.md#62-pod-state-machine): under `checkpointPolicy: sealOnly` and `none`, an idle session stays on its pod and expires with `max_idle_time` at the idle timeout in place of becoming `dormant`, and a suspended session's pod is not released after `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds`. `0146-session-lifecycle` stages the states.
- [§6.4 Pod Filesystem Layout](../../spec/06_warm-pod-model.md#64-pod-filesystem-layout): each slot workspace volume's `sizeLimit` of `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`, 10 GiB by default, `lenny-spool`'s `sizeLimit` of `spoolSizeLimitBytes`, 16 GiB by default per pod, of which the adapter uses `spoolSizeLimitBytes / spoolHeadroomFactor` with each slot's spool cap reserved, and the pod's `ephemeral-storage` request equal to the sum of its disk-backed `sizeLimit` values plus the resource class's own request, 1 GiB in the shipped classes (decision D21), with the spool and export directories under `/adapter/spool/<n>/`. `0118-agent-pod-layout` stages the layout and the volumes themselves, the session volume and its `sessionSizeLimitBytes` included, and `0153-workspace-materialization` stages `spoolSizeLimitBytes`.
- [§7.1 Normal Flow](../../spec/07_session-lifecycle.md#71-normal-flow): the seal, **Derive copy semantics:**, and **Derive restore semantics:**. Under **Seal-and-export invariant:** the seal window is the session's effective `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds`, the runtime revision's value over the pool's, the seal checkpoint runs first with no export grant signed while it runs, and the session's exports finish in the time it leaves in the same window, the whole window under `checkpointPolicy: none`, before `seal_timeout`. A session's terminal pipeline budget stays its seal window plus the release allowance, and the session row's `post_terminal_until` records it at the terminal write (A22 of [`0135-message-delivery`](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#decisions)). The seal window resolves the revision's value, then the pool's, then the gateway flag, each at most the ceiling (A23). Under **Terminal pipeline context:** the budget the deployment checks read becomes `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationCeilingSeconds` plus the release allowance. The runtime field, its ceiling, the claim orphan timeout's computed default, and the readers of the seal window are stated in [`0135-message-delivery`, "Specification changes"](../0135-message-delivery/change.md#specification-changes).
- [§7.3 Retry and Resume](../../spec/07_session-lifecycle.md#73-retry-and-resume): the restore steps through the broker in their order, the exact restore of owner, mode, nanosecond `mtime`, link groups, sparse extents, FIFOs, and symbolic link targets, the identity pass that builds the index, the restore of both of the session's trees with each tree's limit, `restore-complete` after the restore, and the adapter's `restoreConcurrency`.
- [§10.1.7 preStop Hook Drain](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1017-prestop-hook-drain): the single drain cap `evictionCheckpointCapSeconds` in place of the tier table keyed on `last_checkpoint_workspace_bytes`, whose last tier ends at 512 MiB, in the gateway's wait, the agent-pod grace floor, the gateway pod's grace budget, and the BarrierAck floor, each multiplied by `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` because a pod's slots checkpoint one at a time, and removal of the chunked tar model and its reassembly.
- [§17.8.2 Capacity Tier Reference](../../spec/17_deployment-topology.md#1782-capacity-tier-reference), **ResourceQuota tier defaults**: `requests.ephemeral-storage` becomes the tier's `pods` floor times the `ephemeral-storage` request of a one-slot pod at the defaults, 29 GiB before the stores, giving 6Ti, 80Ti, and 430Ti for tiers 1 to 3 in place of 2Ti, 25Ti, and 150Ti, and the sizing formula gains the term `pods × warmCloneBytes` for pools that keep warm stores (decisions D3 and D21).
- [§10.1.8 Rolling Updates and the CheckpointBarrier Protocol](../../spec/10_gateway-internals.md#1018-rolling-updates-and-the-checkpointbarrier-protocol): the barrier hold ends after the capture pass, and the wait for a pod's `CheckpointBarrierAck` is `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod × evictionCheckpointCapSeconds`.
- [§11.2 Budgets and Quotas](../../spec/11_policy-and-controls.md#112-budgets-and-quotas) and [§12.5 Artifact Store](../../spec/12_storage-architecture.md#125-artifact-store): quota per pack, reference-based garbage collection, compaction, and **Workspace deduplication.** §12.5 states the compaction constant `packRewriteThreshold` (0.5). In §11.2, **Hard cap on checkpoint writes.** clamps each declared pack length to `packMaxBytes` in place of `chunk_size_bytes`. §11.2 gains the export reservation, held on the `output_file_exports` row and released by its guarded update of `reservation_released_at` under **Reservation release is exactly-once and guarded.**, with `coordination_generation = $gen` in the guard of every release and state transition, its outstanding term in `Rehydrate` under **The relative decrement is rebuild-safe.**, the draw-down by each confirmed pack, the signing-time headroom check per export, the release of the reservation on every terminal outcome, a new coordinator's takeover without a second reservation, `files.maxConcurrentExports`, and `files.maxQueuedExports`. §12.5 gains `output_files`, `output_file_packs`, and `output_file_exports`, the backstop's release of an export reservation of a terminal session, liveness over both reference tables, a `referenced` pack leaving the allowed set when its last referrer goes, the pack key `(tenant_id, owner_session_id, pack_id)`, the refusal to revive a soft-deleted pack row, `packLeaseSeconds` and its extension, the `FOR SHARE` lock of the reference closure, and the `FOR UPDATE` re-check before a soft delete.
- [§12.5 Artifact Store](../../spec/12_storage-architecture.md#125-artifact-store), [§13.2.3 Checkpoint Capability Model](../../spec/13_security-model.md#1323-checkpoint-capability-model), [§4.7 Runtime Adapter](../../spec/04_system-components.md#47-runtime-adapter), and [§28.3 Registers](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#283-registers): platform chunk sets ([decision D1](#decisions)). §12.5 gains the sources and their owners, the tables `platform_sources`, `platform_packs`, `platform_contributions`, `checkpoint_snapshot_platform_packs`, and `output_file_platform_packs`, the pack keys under `/platform/chunk-sets/` and `/{tenant_id}/chunk-sets/`, liveness by reference and by the latest contribution of a source that is not retired, and the quota rule. §13.2.3 gains the eligibility computed from `coveredStores` and the session's runtime, the allowed set's platform packs, the grants on `CH-SOURCECHUNKS` for a pod with no bind only, the gateway's answer on a set's chunks to a contributing pod with no bind only, and the residual exposure of S8. §4.7 gains the contribution before READY, the matching by chunk ID, and the seeding of the file index at adoption. §28.3 gains the `CH-SOURCECHUNKS` register row and its contract card, and §28.4 a claim-register row.
- [§12.8 Compliance Interfaces](../../spec/12_storage-architecture.md#128-compliance-interfaces): erasure and legal hold over shared packs and over the platform packs a held item references, and the legal-hold escrow of a pack-backed item, a snapshot or an output file, as every pack it references and its row.
- [§13.2.3 Checkpoint Capability Model](../../spec/13_security-model.md#1323-checkpoint-capability-model): the pack key layout, signed `Content-MD5`, `GET` scoping by snapshot, and the constant `packMaxBytes` (32 MiB). The pack nonce makes every pack ID an adapter builds distinct. The gateway refuses a grant for a pack larger than `packMaxBytes` and for a pack key its catalog already holds in any state, soft-deleted rows included, resolves a pack ID against the session's own key first, and answers "already stored" for a pack in the session's allowed set, consulting another session's packs in that set only for that answer. The residual exposure becomes the pool's effective `checkpointGrantWindow` times `packMaxBytes`, counted per session across the `Checkpoint` and `ExportOutputFile` streams, 128 MiB by default and at most 256 MiB, and the paragraph names today's bound of 4 × 16 MiB in place of stating that the exposure is unchanged.
- [§15 External API Surface](../../spec/15_external-api-surface.md#15-external-api-surface): `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/workspace` keeps its contract and returns 404 under `checkpointPolicy: none`, the derive refusal gains the `checkpointing_disabled` reason, and the session envelope reports the effective `checkpointPolicy`.
- [§15.4.3 Runtime Integration Levels](../../spec/15_external-api-surface.md#1543-runtime-integration-levels) and [§28.5.3 Intra-pod](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#2853-intra-pod): `0139-runtime-protocol` replaces the levels with capabilities, and every runtime that does not declare `checkpoint` gets the best-effort snapshot that decision D12 gives a Basic runtime. That design stages the change.
- [§16 Observability](../../spec/16_observability.md#16-observability): the new metrics including the tracker's and `lenny_checkpoint_metadata_only_paths_total`, which `0145-session-isolation` also names, the redefined `CheckpointDurationHigh` alert without its embedded-adapter scope, a pause objective in place of the duration objective, and `CheckpointStale` leaving out sessions under `checkpointPolicy: sealOnly` and `none`.
- [§17.8.1 Operational Defaults — Quick Reference](../../spec/17_deployment-topology.md#1781-operational-defaults--quick-reference): a row for `gateway.checkpointConcurrency` (16) beside the `checkpointGrantWindow` row, a row for `controller.adapterRestoreConcurrency` (8), the `checkpointGrantWindow` row restated as per session with a per-pool maximum of 8 and the gateway flag's maximum of 8, and rows for `packLeaseSeconds` (1800) with its admission rule, `exportTimeoutSeconds` (900), `files.maxConcurrentExports` (2), `files.maxQueuedExports` (8), and `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationCeilingSeconds` (600).
- [§18 Build Sequence](../../spec/18_build-sequence.md#18-build-sequence): the checkpoint benchmark measures the pause and the new-byte rate, and the `SIGSTOP`/`SIGCONT` embedded-adapter deliverable is removed (IC-9).
- [§28.5.3 Intra-pod](../../spec/28_communication-channels.md#2853-intra-pod): the `checkpoint_request` and `checkpoint_complete` rows, which `0139-runtime-protocol` moves from `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` to `CH-MSGSOCK` under its O7 (a). The `fs` request on `CH-SUPERVISE` and its operations are staged by `0145-session-isolation`, and this design relies on `open`, `openpath`, `statat`, `mkfile`, `mkdir`, `mkfifo`, `symlink`, `link`, `utimens`, `chmod`, `inotify`, and `watch`.

## Stored formats and wire contracts this forces

| Surface | Change |
| :-- | :-- |
| `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto` | Pack messages on `Checkpoint`, `CaptureDone`, `SnapshotReady` with the consumed message-id set, `PackStored`, `ReferenceRefused` with the offending pack IDs, restore messages that name packs, and the output-file chunk lists and leased packs on `CheckpointStart` and `Resume`. The `ExportOutputFile` RPC, on the channel `CH-OUTPUTFILE`, with `ExportStart`, `ExportProbe`, `ExportReady`, and `ExportAbort` with its reason, and the pack messages it shares with `Checkpoint`. |
| `CH-MSGSOCK` | `checkpoint_complete` confirms the capture, and `deadlineMs` carries the pause budget. The quiesce frames travel on `CH-MSGSOCK` under RP O7 (a). |
| Postgres | `checkpoint_packs`, keyed on `(tenant_id, owner_session_id, pack_id)`, `checkpoint_snapshots` with `consumed_message_ids`, and `checkpoint_snapshot_packs`, which replace the partial-manifest table and the per-checkpoint chunk rows, and `output_files`, `output_file_packs`, and `output_file_exports` for output files. `output_files` keeps the whole-file `sha256` as metadata and has no verification column. |
| Object keys | `/{tenant_id}/checkpoints/{session_id}/packs/{pack_id}`, and for platform chunk sets `/platform/chunk-sets/{source_id}/packs/{pack_id}` and `/{tenant_id}/chunk-sets/{source_id}/packs/{pack_id}`. |
| Platform chunk sets | The Postgres tables `platform_sources`, `platform_packs`, `platform_contributions`, `checkpoint_snapshot_platform_packs`, and `output_file_platform_packs`. The `CH-SOURCECHUNKS` stream with IC-3's pack messages and `ContributionReady`. The eligible contributions on `CheckpointStart`, `ExportStart`, and `Resume`, and the chunk question a contributing pod asks. The metrics `lenny_platform_chunk_bytes_referenced_total` and `lenny_platform_contribution_seconds`. |
| Pack and manifest formats | Versioned binary formats, with codecs in the adapter and the gateway. The pack footer carries the 128-bit pack nonce. A manifest entry names its tree, `workspace` or `session`, stores its path relative to that tree as a byte string, and records the type (regular file, directory, symbolic link, hard link, FIFO, or socket), every permission bit, the size, a sparse file's data extents, `mtime` in nanoseconds, a symbolic link's target as a byte string, a hard link's link-group leader, the chunk list, and the flags `torn` and `metadata_only`. It records no numeric owner, `atime`, `ctime`, or extended attribute. |
| File index | Each entry holds the identity the walk read in the slot's mount view, from a `statx` relative to a broker-passed directory descriptor, a `statat` reply, or a broker-passed descriptor, and the link count, and the index maps device and inode to the names of each link group. It is stored under `/adapter/spool/<n>/`. |
| Pod specification | Each slot workspace volume's `sizeLimit` is `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`, 10 GiB by default, `lenny-spool`'s is `spoolSizeLimitBytes`, and the pod's `ephemeral-storage` request is the sum of its disk-backed `sizeLimit` values. On pools with `isolationProfile: sandboxed` the gVisor pod-share annotations name every slot workspace volume, every slot session volume, and `lenny-spool`. `0118-agent-pod-layout` renders the rest of the layout. |
| `CH-SUPERVISE` `fs` request | Staged by `0145-session-isolation`. This design uses the operations listed under "Specification sections affected", §28.5.3, `statat` included, with batches of at most 253 paths and 56 KiB and a per-request deadline of `1 s + 4 ms × n`. |
| Pool configuration | `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` with its default of 10 GiB, `checkpointPauseBudgetSeconds`, `checkpointCaptureBudgetBytes`, `checkpointPreCopyRounds`, `checkpointAttemptTimeoutSeconds`, `evictionCheckpointCapSeconds`, `checkpointPolicy`, `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds`, `checkpointChangeTracker`, `checkpointTrackerMaxWatches`, and `exportTimeoutSeconds`. The `PodSet.checkpointGrantWindow` override gains a maximum of 8 in its CRD validation. |
| Runtime definition | `checkpointPolicy`, `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds`, and `checkpointExcludes` ([D5](#decisions)). |
| Constants | `packMaxBytes` (32 MiB), `packRewriteThreshold` (0.5), `reflinkMinBytes` (1 MiB), `racyWindow` (2 s), `bestEffortRereads` (2), `exportSpoolFraction` (0.5), `spoolHeadroomFactor` (2), `pauseEstimateMargin` (1.5), `walkFallbackConcurrency` (2), and the tracker's event cap (16,384 events per swap). The specification states them, and no setting changes them. The gateway refuses a pack grant whose declared length exceeds `packMaxBytes`. |
| Gateway settings | `LENNY_CHECKPOINT_CONCURRENCY`, read by `--checkpoint-concurrency`, 16 by default. `packLeaseSeconds`, 1800 by default, from the chart value `gateway.packLeaseSeconds`. `--checkpoint-grant-window` and `LENNY_CHECKPOINT_GRANT_WINDOW`, refused at startup above 8. |
| Controller and adapter flags | The controller flag `--adapter-restore-concurrency`, which the controller passes to the adapter of every agent pod it creates as `--restore-concurrency`, 8 by default. |
| Chart values | `checkpoint.minIntervalSeconds`, `checkpoint.maxIntervalSeconds`, `gateway.checkpointConcurrency`, `gateway.packLeaseSeconds`, and `controller.adapterRestoreConcurrency`, and the `requests.ephemeral-storage` defaults of the agent namespaces' ResourceQuota (IC-7). |
| Session envelope | The effective `checkpointPolicy`. |
| `FinalizeWorkspace` and `Resume` in `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto`, and the session record | The session's `checkpointExcludes` list, composed at the post-chain point and stored on the session record. |
| Metrics | `lenny_checkpoint_metadata_only_paths_total`, which `0145-session-isolation` also names, `lenny_checkpoint_pause_seconds`, `lenny_checkpoint_new_bytes_total`, `lenny_checkpoint_precopy_rounds`, `lenny_checkpoint_torn_files_total`, `lenny_checkpoint_consistency_downgrade_total`, `lenny_checkpoint_tracker_state`, `lenny_checkpoint_tracker_fallback_total` with the reasons `overflow` and `event_cap` among them, `lenny_checkpoint_tracker_watches`, and `lenny_checkpoint_queue_wait_seconds`, the time an attempt waited in the pod's checkpoint queue. |

The platform has no deployments, so the old format gets no migration or dual-read path.

## Changes to Lenny's documentation

The documentation changes land with the spec changes, after the owner approves the design. Published pages cite no spec section numbers, so the pages below state behavior and settings directly.

### New page: `docs/operator-guide/checkpointing.md`

A new Operator Guide page, titled "Checkpointing", sits after "Observability" in the navigation. It covers these topics:

1. **How a checkpoint runs.** The pre-copy, capture, and upload passes, which pass pauses a quiescing runtime, why the first checkpoint of a session takes longer without pausing longer, and how a restore verifies what it writes. What a restore reproduces exactly, which includes owner, mode, `mtime`, hard links, and FIFOs, and what it cannot: the content of entries the session's own user cannot read or list, sockets, and extended attributes.
2. **Consistency by the `checkpoint` capability.** A runtime that declares `checkpoint` gets a consistent snapshot and pauses for the capture. Every other runtime gets a best-effort snapshot with no pause. A pod's slots checkpoint one at a time. The platform never pauses a runtime by force.
3. **Benchmarks.** The steady and package-install pause tables for Chromium and LLVM, the per-file cost table, the tracker's effect on file creation and deletion, and restore times, from "Benchmarks" and "Re-validation through the broker" in this design, with the figures through the broker as the current ones. The page states the date, the node types, the component versions, and the harness's quiesce stand-in. It states that a deployer's own numbers depend on CPU, disk, and workspace layout, and it names the metrics that measure them in production.
4. **Operator levers.** `checkpointPolicy`, `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` and the deployer's interval bounds, `checkpointChangeTracker`, `checkpointPauseBudgetSeconds`, `checkpointPreCopyRounds`, `checkpointCaptureBudgetBytes`, `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` and `spoolSizeLimitBytes` with the `ephemeral-storage` and ResourceQuota they cost, CPU allocation through the resource class, and a pool with `isolationProfile: standard` on nodes dedicated to one tenant, each with its measured effect and its cost, as in "Levers". The page states that runc shares the host kernel and gives no protection against kernel exploits, that node separation, user namespaces, and dedicated tenancy bound its blast radius, and that the pool needs `allowStandardIsolation: true`, the operator's explicit opt-in.
5. **Node configuration for deployers.** For gVisor, the containerd `pod_annotations = ['dev.gvisor.*']` setting that lets the mount hints on the slot workspace and session volumes and the spool volume reach runsc, with the measured gain and the symptom of its absence (the tracker stays off and the walk pause applies). The statement that the tracker stays off on gVisor's default mount because Chromium-sized trees exhaust the sandbox's descriptors. The statements that `--file-access-mounts=exclusive` stops kubelet updates from reaching the sandbox and must not be set, and that `--platform=kvm` makes checkpoints slower. For Kata, `emptydir_mode = "block-plain"`, which gives the lowest Kata pause and needs the pool's dedicated `fsGroup`, `kernel_params` for the guest watch limit, which the tracker needs on both of Kata's file systems, and `virtio_fs_cache = "metadata"` for deployers who keep the shared file system. For runc, the `fs.inotify.max_user_watches` and `fs.inotify.max_user_instances` sysctls. Node disk choice and gp3 IOPS for the first checkpoint and for restore.
6. **Runtime author and tenant levers.** Declaring the `checkpoint` capability or leaving it out, pointing the harness's state directory at `/session` so that it is checkpointed with the workspace, the git maintenance settings of the rendered system configuration and how a runtime overrides them at the cost of its own checkpoints, `checkpointPolicy` and `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` on the Runtime, `checkpointExcludes` and the revision a change to it creates, a stricter `checkpointPolicy`, a `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` at or above the base's interval, and appended `checkpointExcludes` on a derived runtime, and asking the operator for a dedicated pool.
7. **Platform chunk sets.** What a set holds, which sources the platform and a tenant own, that only a never-launched pod, one to which no session has bound, contributes and only before READY, which sources a session is eligible for, that matching is by content, that `securitySettings.storage.platformChunkReuse` turns the sets off for a tenant and is `false` in the `regulated` template, that operator-source chunks are stored under the deployment key even for a tenant with its own key, the residual risks of IC-4, "Platform chunk sets", the purge procedure of IC-6, and the contributing pod's longer warm-up.
8. **Reference ([decision D13](#decisions)).** Tables that state every item of this design a deployer, an operator, a runtime author, or a tenant meets, each with its default and its bounds:
   - Pool settings: `checkpointPolicy` (`full`), `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` (the gateway default of 600 s, clamped to `checkpoint.minIntervalSeconds` 60 and `checkpoint.maxIntervalSeconds` 3600), `checkpointPauseBudgetSeconds` (10, below 60), `checkpointPreCopyRounds` (3), `checkpointCaptureBudgetBytes` (512 MiB), `checkpointAttemptTimeoutSeconds` (300), `evictionCheckpointCapSeconds` (90), `checkpointChangeTracker` (`auto`), `checkpointTrackerMaxWatches` (262,144), `exportTimeoutSeconds` (900), `checkpointGrantWindow` (the gateway's 4, at most 8), `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` (10 GiB), `sessionSizeLimitBytes` (256 MiB), `spoolSizeLimitBytes` (16 GiB per pod), and `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` (300, at most `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationCeilingSeconds`, 600).
   - Runtime fields: `checkpointPolicy`, `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds`, `checkpointExcludes` (empty), and `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds`, with what a tenant may set on a derived runtime.
   - Chart values and gateway settings: `checkpoint.minIntervalSeconds`, `checkpoint.maxIntervalSeconds`, `gateway.checkpointConcurrency` (16), `gateway.packLeaseSeconds` (1800, at least twice the larger of the attempt and export timeouts), `gateway.checkpointGrantWindow` (4, at most 8), `controller.adapterRestoreConcurrency` (8), `files.maxConcurrentExports` (2), `files.maxQueuedExports` (8), and the ResourceQuota's `requests.ephemeral-storage` defaults per tier (6Ti, 80Ti, and 430Ti).
   - Constants: `packMaxBytes` (32 MiB), `packRewriteThreshold` (0.5), `reflinkMinBytes` (1 MiB), `racyWindow` (2 s), `bestEffortRereads` (2), `exportSpoolFraction` (0.5), `spoolHeadroomFactor` (2), `pauseEstimateMargin` (1.5), `walkFallbackConcurrency` (2), the 16 walker threads, and the tracker's event cap of 16,384 events per swap.
   - Sizing: the pod's `ephemeral-storage` request equals the sum of its disk-backed `sizeLimit` values plus the resource class's own `requests.ephemeral-storage`, 1 GiB in the shipped classes and configurable per class, 29 GiB for a one-slot pod at the defaults before the warm stores, and 12 GiB more per further slot. The adapter uses half of the spool, reserves each slot's spool cap from that half, and shares the rest as upload staging.
   - Node requirements: Linux 5.18 or later for `FICLONE` between a slot volume and the spool, and XFS with `reflink=1` or Btrfs on the kubelet's file system for reflink mode, with copy or read mode elsewhere; Linux 5.9 or later for the broker; the containerd `pod_annotations = ['dev.gvisor.*']` setting for the gVisor tracker and pod-share mounts; Kata's `kernel_params` watch limit, `emptydir_mode = "block-plain"`, and the pool's dedicated `fsGroup` there; the runc inotify sysctls; and user namespaces on runc pools, which give each pod its own watch budget.
   - Limits: the pause grows with the file count without a tracker; a restore still downloads the whole workspace; a file of mode 0000, a directory its owner cannot list, a socket, and extended attributes are not restored with content; gVisor restores a sparse file without holes; the tracker stays off on gVisor's default mount; a flat directory of 1,000,000 entries falls to best effort on gVisor's default mount and on Kata's shared file system; and a pod's slots checkpoint one at a time, so one slot's checkpoint can delay another's.
   - Failure modes and what the operator sees: a best-effort downgrade (`lenny_checkpoint_consistency_downgrade_total`), a torn file, a tracker fallback by reason (`overflow`, `event_cap`, a failed watch, a missed barrier, a failed cross-check), `precopy_required`, an attempt that times out in the stagger queue, a dead slot whose checkpoint fails and whose session resumes from its latest snapshot, a size probe over `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`, the disk watch failing a slot at `adapter.diskWatchThresholdPercent` of a volume's `sizeLimit`, 90% by default, a pod evicted for a volume over its `sizeLimit`, `ReferenceRefused`, the `ExportAbort` reasons, `seal_timeout`, `pod_evicted`, `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` for a download under `none`, and `422 DERIVE_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE` with `checkpointing_disabled`.
   - Policy effects: under `sealOnly` and `none` a pod failure resumes the session without its workspace, an idle session stays on its pod and expires at the idle timeout, and a suspended session keeps its pod.
   - Runtime-facing rules: declare `checkpoint` to receive `checkpoint_request` with `deadlineMs` set to the pause budget on `CH-MSGSOCK`, stop writing, answer `checkpoint_ready`, and continue on `checkpoint_complete`, which now means captured; a runtime resumes on its own 60 s after `checkpoint_ready`; a runtime that does not declare `checkpoint` is captured best-effort while it runs and is never stopped; put harness state in `/session`; list regenerable directories in `checkpointExcludes`; leave the reserved names `.lenny-build`, `.lenny-prestage`, `.lenny-overlay-<id>`, and `.lenny-discard-<id>` at the top of `/workspace` alone, because checkpoints skip them, and keep nothing in a `lost+found` directory at the root of `/workspace` or `/session`, which checkpoints and downloads skip; expect `metadata_only` warnings for entries the session's own user cannot read; and leave automatic git maintenance off, or accept that a repack uploads the repository's history at the next checkpoint.
9. **Monitoring.** `lenny_checkpoint_pause_seconds`, `lenny_checkpoint_consistency_downgrade_total`, `lenny_checkpoint_torn_files_total`, `lenny_checkpoint_new_bytes_total`, `lenny_checkpoint_precopy_rounds`, `lenny_checkpoint_queue_wait_seconds`, `lenny_checkpoint_metadata_only_paths_total`, the tracker metrics `lenny_checkpoint_tracker_state` (the tracker's state and the effective `checkpointChangeTracker` value per slot), `lenny_checkpoint_tracker_fallback_total` (each fallback to the walk, by the reasons `overflow`, `event_cap`, a failed watch, a missed barrier, and a failed cross-check), and `lenny_checkpoint_tracker_watches` (the watches each tracker holds, against `checkpointTrackerMaxWatches` and the node's inotify limits), `lenny_platform_chunk_bytes_referenced_total`, `lenny_platform_contribution_seconds`, and what each alert means.
