| Page | Change |
| :-- | :-- |
| `docs/operator-guide/configuration.md` | The pool example replaces the undefined `checkpointPolicy: {enabled, intervalSeconds}` block with `checkpointPolicy: full`, `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds: 600`, and `checkpointChangeTracker: auto`, and links to the checkpointing page. |
| `docs/reference/configuration.md` | The "Checkpointing configuration" table drops `pool.checkpointing.enabled`, `pool.checkpointing.interval`, and `pool.checkpointing.workspaceSizeLimitBytes`, and lists every pool and Runtime field in "Stored formats and wire contracts this forces" with its default and bounds, plus the chart values `checkpoint.minIntervalSeconds`, `checkpoint.maxIntervalSeconds`, `gateway.checkpointConcurrency`, and `controller.adapterRestoreConcurrency`. The Runtime row `capabilities.checkpoint` is replaced by a pointer to the `"checkpoint"` entry of `lifecycle_support`. |
| `docs/reference/metrics.md` and `docs/operator-guide/observability.md` | The checkpoint metrics tables add the new metrics. The "Checkpoint duration (100MB)" objective becomes a pause objective, as in §16. |
| `docs/alerting/rules.yaml` and `docs/alerting/openslo.yaml` | `CheckpointDurationHigh` and `CheckpointDurationBurnRate` measure `lenny_checkpoint_pause_seconds`. `CheckpointStale` leaves out sessions whose effective `checkpointPolicy` is `sealOnly` or `none` and measures each session against its own interval. |
| `docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md` | "Checkpoint Failures" drops the `.lennyignore` and size-limit advice. It adds best-effort downgrades and what lowers them, the tracker's state by file system type and the node setting each state points to, tracker fallbacks by reason, and a quiescing runtime that is slow to send `checkpoint_ready`. |
| `docs/runbooks/checkpoint-stale.md` | The runbook measures staleness against each session's effective interval, leaves out sessions whose effective `checkpointPolicy` is `sealOnly` or `none`, and explains that a large workspace's first checkpoint can span several attempts. |
| `docs/runtime-author-guide/lifecycle.md` | The "Checkpointing" section states that a runtime that does not declare `checkpoint` gets a best-effort snapshot, in capability terms as `0139-runtime-protocol` restates the page. The example of a runtime that declares `checkpoint` carries `deadlineMs` as the pause budget, `checkpoint_complete` arrives once the capture ends, and the section gives the measured pause range and links to the checkpointing page. It states that the platform does not pause a runtime that does not quiesce for a checkpoint (IC-9), and the quarantine freeze of [`0128-forensics-evidence-preservation`](../0128-forensics-evidence-preservation/change.md#freezing-the-agent) stays a separate statement on that page, and it describes `checkpointExcludes` and `checkpointPolicy` on the Runtime. The sentence that a late `checkpoint_ready` sets a `checkpointStuck` health flag is removed, because IC-9 removes the flag. |
| `docs/runtime-author-guide/integration-levels.md` | `0139-runtime-protocol` replaces the page with capabilities. Its checkpoint statement gives every runtime without `checkpoint` a best-effort snapshot, and the handshake example's default deadline is the pause budget. The sentence that flags `checkpointStuck` after a late `checkpoint_ready` is removed (IC-9). |
| `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md` | `checkpoint_complete` means the snapshot is captured, `deadlineMs` carries the pause budget, and the quiesce frames travel on the runtime connection. |
| `docs/getting-started/concepts.md` and `docs/getting-started/architecture.md` | The checkpoint description changes from a tar of the slot to incremental packs and a manifest, and the exception for checkpoint chunk objects to gateway-mediated file delivery, under which the pod reaches object storage directly with presigned grants, names packs. |
| `docs/api/internal.md` | The `Checkpoint` stream messages follow IC-3. |
| `docs/client-guide/session-lifecycle.md` | The session envelope reports the effective `checkpointPolicy`, and the page states which policies resume with `workspaceLost: true` and that a session under `sealOnly` or `none` expires at the idle timeout in place of becoming dormant. |
| `docs/operator-guide/namespace-and-isolation.md` | The isolation profile summary links to the checkpointing page for the checkpoint cost of each profile. |
| `docs/operator-guide/scaling.md` and `docs/reference/configuration.md` | `capacityPlanning.avgWorkspaceSizeMB` estimates the first checkpoint, and checkpoint bandwidth after it follows the rate of changed bytes. The agent pod's `ephemeral-storage` request is the sum of its disk-backed `sizeLimit` values plus the resource class's own request, 1 GiB in the shipped classes, 29 GiB for a one-slot pod at the defaults before the warm stores, and the ResourceQuota's `requests.ephemeral-storage` defaults are 6Ti, 80Ti, and 430Ti for tiers 1 to 3. |
| `docs/reference/glossary.md` | Entries for pre-copy, capture pass, pause, change tracker, pack, best-effort snapshot, metadata-only entry, and platform chunk set. |
| `docs/operator-guide/multi-tenancy.md` and the operator page on templates of [`0150-tenant-security-settings`](../0150-tenant-security-settings/change.md) | `storage.platformChunkReuse`, its template defaults, and the deployment key for operator-source chunks. |

## Tests

### Tier 1: unit

- Chunk boundaries are deterministic, and an insertion changes only nearby chunks.
- Pack and manifest codecs round-trip and reject truncated or reordered input.
- The racy rule re-reads an entry whose `mtime` falls inside the window.
- The reference closure check rejects a foreign pack, a pack from the source's later snapshot, and an expired `unreferenced` pack.
- Convergence: over budget, the adapter takes another round and falls back to best-effort after the last one.
- Policy resolution picks the stricter value, and a derived runtime cannot loosen its base's policy.
- Eligibility holds the stores of `coveredStores` and the session's own runtime's assets, never a withheld store, and is empty for a tenant whose `platformChunkReuse` is `false`.
- A file under 1 MiB and a larger file staged from the same commit on two pods produce the same chunk IDs, and a one-byte change matches no chunk of the set.
- Interval resolution prefers the pool, then the Runtime, then the gateway default, and clamps to the deployer's bounds.
- The pause estimate sums the change-source cost, the per-file cost, and the byte cost, adds the tracker backlog, counts reflinked bytes at the clone cost, and takes the slowest rate observed in the attempt's rounds and the session's earlier captures on the pod. The adapter sends `checkpoint_request` when 1.5 times the estimate equals the budget and takes another round when it exceeds it by any amount.
- The tracker's swap returns every path changed before the barrier file, an overflow forces the next capture to walk, and a failed watch keeps the tracker degraded across swaps.
- The gateway's pack grant check refuses a declared length of zero or above `packMaxBytes` and accepts a length equal to `packMaxBytes`.
- The gateway reads `checkpointConcurrency` from `LENNY_CHECKPOINT_CONCURRENCY`, 16 when unset, and the controller's pod builder renders `--restore-concurrency` on the adapter from `--adapter-restore-concurrency`.
- `checkpointChangeTracker: auto` resolves from the file system type that `fstatfs` reports on the workspace broker root: to on for a node file system under runc, for `ext4` on a Kata block volume, for `virtiofs` on Kata's shared file system, and for `tmpfs` under gVisor's pod-share mount hint, and to off for `9p`, any other `fuse` file system, and an unknown type.
- The parallel stat walk calls `statx` only on single names `getdents` returned and refuses `.`, `..`, an empty name, and a name with `/` or a NUL byte; refuses a result whose device or mount ID differs from the pinned root's or that lacks a mount ID; opens subdirectories with `openat2` and the broker's resolution flags and refuses a descriptor whose inode differs from the entry's `statx`; falls back to `statat` and broker `open` per directory on `EACCES` only, with at most `walkFallbackConcurrency` fallback requests outstanding; splits a large directory's names into chunks of 256; and sends at most 253 paths and 56 KiB per request with a deadline of `1 s + 4 ms × n`.
- A walk whose estimate alone exceeds the pause budget sends the capture to the best-effort path, and the estimate counts the first `getdents` of a large directory.
- The tracker treats the 16,384th event of a swap interval as an overflow, keeps walking at every capture after an overflow until a walk completes, and walks at the next capture when the barrier event does not arrive.
- The checkpoint queue runs at most one attempt per pod, starts the next in arrival order when one ends, charges the wait to the waiting attempt's deadline, fails an attempt whose deadline passes in the queue, queues a seal like any attempt, and lets an interrupt of a queued slot proceed.
- The walk and the tracker skip `.lenny-canary`, `.lenny-build`, `.lenny-prestage`, the recorded name of an overlay in progress, and `.lenny-discard-<id>` at the top of `/workspace`, and a `lost+found` directory at the root of either tree. A restore creates no `lost+found` and writes nothing into one, and a download leaves it out.
- The tracker sets no watch on the reserved names, on a `checkpointExcludes` directory, or on a `metadata_only` directory, and the disk watch measures those directories by a walk at each 10 s interval, and the whole volume by the walk while the tracker is degraded, except on a volume that a project quota bounds, which it reads with `fstatfs` alone.
- The BarrierAck floor refuses a pool whose `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` is below `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod × evictionCheckpointCapSeconds`, and the gateway's wait for a pod's `CheckpointBarrierAck` and its preStop wait per pod are that product.
- Eligibility gives a session of a tenant-derived runtime its base's assets source and its own runtime's tenant-owned assets source, an owned pod contributes the base's asset files to the base's set and the tenant's appended files to the tenant-owned set, and a contribution from a staged tree of a tenant's warm-list entry goes to the tenant-owned set of that URL, never to the platform-owned set of the same URL.
- A FIFO whose descriptor fails the device check under gVisor's default mount is recorded as `metadata_only` from its listing, and any other entry that fails the check is refused.
- The broker client refuses each hostile reply of S6: a descriptor whose device or mount ID differs from the broker root's, a wrong type or access mode, a regular file over the size bound, a restore descriptor that is not `O_WRONLY` with size 0 and one link, a descriptor count that differs from the number of zero error numbers, a truncated control message, a wrong reply ID, an unknown member, an overlong line, and a reply past the deadline, which marks the slot dead. An `O_PATH` descriptor for a regular file yields a metadata-only entry and no read.
- The path encoder sends and the manifest codec stores a path holding invalid UTF-8 and bytes of `0x80` and above unchanged.
- The manifest codec round-trips every entry type, a link group, a sparse file's extents, `mtime` in nanoseconds, a symbolic link target that is not UTF-8, and the `metadata_only` flag, and rejects a hard link whose leader is missing or follows it.
- Link groups: a dirty path whose link count is 2 marks the other name of its inode dirty, and an inode whose link count exceeds the names the index holds is checked by identity at every capture.
- The spool accounting counts each file at the larger of its logical and allocated size, counts a reflink clone and a copy at full size, and never calls `statfs`. A clone or a copy larger than the session's remaining share is not made, and the file is read in place. The reflink probe resolves reflink, copy, and read modes from the results of `FICLONE`, FIEMAP, and `copy_file_range`, and copy mode never claims sharing. The controller renders `lenny-spool`'s `sizeLimit` as `spoolSizeLimitBytes`, each slot workspace volume's as `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` with a default of 10 GiB, and the pod's `ephemeral-storage` request as the sum of its disk-backed `sizeLimit` values. The adapter uses at most `spoolSizeLimitBytes / spoolHeadroomFactor`, reserves each slot's spool cap from it, and pool admission refuses a pool whose usable share does not hold every slot's cap plus the staging floor.
- An export in copy mode of a file within the remaining export share is frozen by `copy_file_range` and counted at full size, and a larger file is read in place with the identity check.
- A capture of a dead slot fails without reading through the adapter's own mount.
- The manifest codec records each entry's tree, a download renders only the `workspace` tree, and a restore writes both trees beneath their own broker roots with each tree's size limit.
- A write to `checkpointPolicy` or `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` creates no revision and reaches the next session on every revision in the traffic. A write to `checkpointExcludes` creates a revision with the same pod hash, and a session's capture leaves out the selected revision's list. A tenant's `checkpointExcludes` on a derived runtime is appended to the selected base revision's list. A resume, including one that moves revision, sends the stored list.
- A tenant's `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` below the base's interval is refused at write, one at or above it is accepted and sets the session's interval, and a later base change that raises the base's interval above the tenant's gives the next session the base's interval.
- `pkg/checkpoint`'s `Level` has no `embedded` value, and no adapter code path sends `SIGSTOP` to a runtime for a checkpoint (IC-9).
- Packs: two packs built from the same chunks carry different nonces and pack IDs, and the nonce round-trips through the pack codec.
- Exports: a resend of a pack in the session's allowed set after a lost `PackCommitted` receives `PackStored` and no grant, a file chunked again after a failure gets new pack IDs and new grants, a retry references the chunks of confirmed packs under lease and rebuilds no pack, the gateway resolves the session's own key before the allowed set's packs another session owns and answers those only with `PackStored`, and the same pack ID under another session's key outside the allowed set receives a grant for the writing session's own key. A `referenced` pack whose last snapshot rotated away and that no output file references is outside the allowed set, and a snapshot that still references it receives `ReferenceRefused`. A chunk enters the dedup set only after `PackCommitted`, and `ReferenceRefused` evicts the named packs' chunks. `ExportReady` is refused for non-contiguous offsets, a chunk over 4 MiB, a chunk length that differs from the pack footer, and a total that differs from `ExportProbe` or exceeds the limit. The export reservation is drawn down by each confirmed pack, the signing-time headroom check refuses a grant past the reservation and the headroom when the probe reported zero new bytes, and success, failure, `exportTimeoutSeconds`, `seal_timeout`, `REJECT`, and pod loss each release the rest exactly once through the guarded update of `reservation_released_at`, also when two of them race. `Rehydrate` adds `reserved_bytes − confirmed_bytes` over the unreleased `output_file_exports` rows. A new coordinator takes over an unreleased reservation without reserving again. `exportTimeoutSeconds` counts from `started_at`, and a part waiting in the export queue fails at the same span from `first_frame_at`. At the seal, no export grant is signed while the seal checkpoint runs, an export that continues after it references the chunks the seal confirmed, each part still exporting or waiting at the end of the session's seal window fails with `seal_timeout`, and under `checkpointPolicy: none` the exports get the whole window. An adapter at its descriptor cap relays a further part with `unopened: true`, the gateway refuses exactly the marked parts with `too_many_exports`, and the adapter frees a waiting part's descriptor and clone at its `output_failed`. An update of an export record that carries another generation changes nothing. `ExportAbort` carries `pack_empty`, `pack_too_large`, and `pack_key_held` for the matching `PackReady`. `packLeaseSeconds` is extended by `SnapshotReady`, `ExportReady`, and `PackStored`, and pool admission refuses a `packLeaseSeconds` below twice the larger of the attempt and export timeouts. Export clones stop at `exportSpoolFraction` of the spool cap, the clones of waiting parts count against it, and a file over the remaining share is read in place without a clone. The grant window counts both streams per session, honors the pool override up to 8, refuses a gateway `--checkpoint-grant-window` above 8 at startup, and after a handoff counts the whole window as outstanding until the grant TTL has passed since the fence acknowledgement. An adapter that acknowledges a fence cancels the previous generation's open `Checkpoint` and `ExportOutputFile` streams. An adapter writes contiguous chunk offsets in file order and a whole-file SHA-256 that matches the file it read. A snapshot's `consumed_message_ids` round-trips in report order.

### Tier 2: component

- Pool admission accepts an interval outside the deployer's bounds only as a clamped value.

### Tier 3: contract

- `FinalizeWorkspace` and `Resume` carry the session's `checkpointExcludes` list, and a capture after either leaves the listed directories out.
- `Checkpoint` message order and every abort arm.
- `checkpoint_complete` is sent once the capture ends, before any pack of the upload pass.
- A `PackReady` whose `length` exceeds `packMaxBytes` receives `CheckpointAbort`, and no `PackGrant` is sent.
- `ExportOutputFile` message order, `ExportAbort` with each reason, `PackStored` and `ReferenceRefused` on both streams, the output-file chunk lists and leased packs on `CheckpointStart` and `Resume`, and the generation stamp on `CH-OUTPUTFILE`, which rejects a stream from a stale coordinator.
- The `PodSet` CRD refuses a `checkpointGrantWindow` above 8.

### Tier 4: integration

- A checkpoint and restore round trip against MinIO, with byte-for-byte comparison.
- A second checkpoint after a one-file change uploads one pack.
- A timed-out attempt's packs are reused by the next attempt.
- A derive references packs without copies, and garbage collection keeps them while the derived snapshot lives.
- Quota is charged once per pack and released at deletion.
- Under an effective `checkpointPolicy: sealOnly`, no checkpoint runs during the session, the seal runs, and a pod failure resumes with `conversation_only`.
- Under an effective `checkpointPolicy: none`, the seal does not run, `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/workspace` returns 404, and a derive with `from: latest` is refused.
- Under an effective `checkpointPolicy` of `sealOnly` or `none`, an idle session keeps its pod until `maxClientIdleSeconds` passes with no activity, then expires with `max_idle_time`, and under `sealOnly` its seal runs. It never enters `dormant`.
- Two pools with different intervals are checkpointed on their own schedules, and `CheckpointStale` measures each against its own interval.
- A runtime that does not declare `checkpoint` in its handshake receives no `checkpoint_request` and is captured on the best-effort path.
- A session whose runtime writes its state under `/session` resumes on another pod with that state and its workspace from one snapshot, and the workspace download holds no `/session` entry.
- On a pod with two slots whose checkpoints come due together, the second slot's attempt starts only after the first ends, and each slot's pause matches its pause alone.
- A checkpoint of a workspace with a plan repository runs `capture-copy` before pre-copy, and a restore onto a pod with a different store runs `restore-complete`, after which `git fsck` passes. Broker requests proceed while a helper run is in progress.
- A session on a tenant-derived runtime whose base revision excludes `node_modules/` and whose tenant appends `dist/` is captured without either directory, and after a resume onto another pod its next capture still leaves both out.
- With the watch limit set below the workspace's directory count, the tracker reports degraded, every capture walks, and a verification walk finds no missed change.
- On a pod with two slots, each slot's checkpoint reads files of mode 0600 and 0400, `mkstemp` files, and the contents of directories of mode 0700 and 0500. A restore onto another pod and another slot index reproduces owner, mode (0000, 0400, 0500, and 0300 included), nanosecond `mtime`, hard links, sparse extents, FIFOs, and symbolic links, including one whose target is absolute, and records and reports a file of mode 0000 and a directory of mode 0300 as `metadata_only`. The first pre-copy after the restore reads no file.
- A symbolic link in one slot's tree that names another slot's volume is not followed by any pass, and the other slot's checkpoint is unchanged.
- The tracker's barrier file is created and written through the broker, a write through one name of a hard-linked file reaches the next capture under both names, and a swap whose barrier event does not arrive walks.
- A slot whose supervisor is killed during a checkpoint is dead, the checkpoint fails, and the session resumes on another pod from its latest snapshot.
- A changed file larger than the session's remaining spool share is read in place on a reflink volume, and no clone exceeds the share, so the kubelet does not evict the pod.
- The slot release runs its Sweep only after the session's seal checkpoint and exports have ended.
- A directory created after the tracker started, and the files written into it before its watch existed, are in the next capture.
- A running `git repack` leaves no `tmp_pack_*` file in the snapshot, and the restored repository passes `git fsck`.
- A derive with `from: latest` charges nothing to the tenant's storage counter, and a derive of a source under an effective `checkpointPolicy: none` is refused with `checkpointing_disabled`.
- A security hold on a session copies every pack of each held snapshot to escrow. Rotation, pack reclamation, `DeleteByUser`, and `DeleteByTenant` call `EnsureCopied` first, and a copy failure refuses the deletion. After the originals are deleted, the escrowed snapshot restores byte for byte.
- An export of a file that a checkpoint captured uploads only changed chunks, a second export of an unchanged file uploads no pack, and a `Range` download reads the recorded pack offsets and verifies each chunk. A corrupted pack fails the read of the chunk it holds under S4. A full download and a `206` response carry the file's `Repr-Digest`, and the scanner's whole-file read of a file whose stored bytes differ from the hash refuses the part on the `REJECT` path.
- A security hold with `include: artifacts` copies an output file's packs and its `output_files` row to escrow, and the deletion after a `REJECT` calls `EnsureCopied` first. A forced tenant deletion under a legal hold escrows every pack of a held snapshot and a held output file with their rows.
- A pack that a rotated snapshot shared with an output file stays until the output file's retention ends.
- A workspace download whose manifest's entry sizes sum past `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` fails.
- Two never-launched pods that stage the same commit contribute once between them, apart from a race that leaves at most one pod's duplicate packs, which garbage collection reclaims. A session that adopts the staged tree uploads only the files it changed on its first checkpoint, its first pre-copy reads no unchanged adopted file, and a restore onto another pod is byte for byte. A derive keeps the platform references, and garbage collection keeps a referenced platform pack after its source retires.
- An asset file the runtime copies into `/workspace` is referenced from the runtime's assets set and not uploaded.
- With `platformChunkReuse: false`, a session's snapshots reference no platform pack, and a tenant-owned source receives no contribution.

### Tier 5: e2e on Kind

- A 5 GiB workspace checkpoints with a pause under budget and restores on a new pod.
- A drain checkpoint fits `evictionCheckpointCapSeconds`.
- The rendered workspace volume carries `sizeLimit`.
- With `gateway.checkpointConcurrency` and `controller.adapterRestoreConcurrency` set in the chart, the gateway Deployment carries `LENNY_CHECKPOINT_CONCURRENCY` with that value, and the adapter of a new agent pod carries `--restore-concurrency` with that value.
- A runtime that does not declare `checkpoint` is checkpointed on the best-effort path, its process is never stopped, and its pod renders no liveness probe (IC-9).
- The rendered pod mounts `slot-<n>-session` at `/session`, carries the pod-share annotations on the workspace and session volumes and `lenny-spool` on a `sandboxed` pool, gives `lenny-spool` the `sizeLimit` of `spoolSizeLimitBytes`, and requests `ephemeral-storage` equal to the sum of its disk-backed `sizeLimit` values. A namespace at the tier 1 ResourceQuota default admits the tier's `pods` floor of one-slot pods at the defaults.

### Tier 6: e2e in the cloud

- The pause, the tracker, and restore for a pod with the adapter as a sidecar and a slot container per slot under runc, gVisor, and Kata, through the file-descriptor broker.
- The tracker on a node for pools with `isolationProfile: sandboxed`, whose containerd handler drops `dev.gvisor.*` annotations, stays off under `checkpointChangeTracker: auto`, and on a node that passes them it runs on every slot workspace volume and reports no fallback.
- The supervisor-held tracker sees every change made by the slot's runtime on every configuration where `checkpointChangeTracker: auto` turns it on, Kata's shared file system included, and `auto` leaves it off on `9p`.
- The parallel stat walk misses no change and matches the slot's own view on every runtime, and its broker fallback on a tree the adapter cannot search keeps every request within its deadline on Kata's shared file system.
- On runc nodes with user namespaces, the trackers of the same slot index on several pods draw on separate watch budgets.
- Spool fills up to the adapter's usable share on gVisor's default mount do not evict the pod with `sizeLimit` at `spoolSizeLimitBytes`.
- The reflink probe for each pair of a slot workspace volume and `lenny-spool`, on each profile: reflink mode under runc on XFS with `reflink=1`, copy mode under gVisor with a full copy from a broker-passed descriptor, and the mode Kata's volumes give.
- Clones and copies up to every slot's spool cap on `lenny-spool` with its rendered `sizeLimit` do not evict the pod.
- Kata with `emptydir_mode = "block-plain"` under the kubelet's `emptyDir` size accounting.

### Tier 7: load

- Concurrent git operations during best-effort captures produce restorable repositories, and the attempt never fails on a changed file.
- An interrupt during the upload pass is acknowledged within the interrupt deadline.
- Concurrent attempts per replica with one slow session.
- A checkpoint and an export of one session never hold more than the pool's effective window of grants together, and the capture pass pauses export reading, hashing, and compression while export pack uploads continue.
- A reference closure racing pack reclamation of the same pack either commits the reference with the pack live or is refused, and never references a soft-deleted pack.
- A writer creating 10,000 files per second during the capture keeps the pause within the budget or ends on the best-effort path, with no missed change in either case.
- The items of "Benchmarks", "Validation owed", through the broker on the session isolation layout, with pods of one, two, and four slots and the checkpoint stagger.
- A flat directory of 1,000,000 entries ends on the best-effort path where its walk exceeds the budget, with no request past its deadline and no slot declared dead.

### Tier 8: chaos

- An adapter crash in each pass, a gateway failover during finalization, and a lease expiry for orphaned packs.
- A coordinator handoff during an export: the adapter cancels the previous generation's streams at the fence acknowledgement, the new coordinator signs no grant for the session until the grant TTL has passed since that acknowledgement, takes over the export's reservation from its `output_file_exports` row, reopens `ExportOutputFile`, and the adapter reuses its clone and confirmed packs. The previous coordinator, still running, treats its cancelled stream as no failure: it releases no reservation, changes no export record, and sends no `output_failed`, and an update it attempts with its own generation afterwards changes no row. A pod loss during an export releases the reservation and leaves the confirmed packs under lease. A Redis loss during an export followed by `Rehydrate` keeps the outstanding export reservation in the counter.
- A drain whose eviction checkpoint runs while an export uploads: no export grant is signed until the checkpoint ends, and an export still running, or a part still waiting in the export queue, at the end of the checkpoint stage fails with `pod_evicted`.

### Tier 9: security

- A pod that names a foreign pack ID is refused.
- A pod that declares a pack larger than `packMaxBytes` receives no grant, and the attempt aborts.
- A `PUT` for an existing pack ID is refused, including one whose row is soft-deleted and awaiting the tombstone sweep.
- A `GET` outside the snapshot's packs is refused.
- A substituted pack is detected at restore.
- An `ExportReady` that names a pack outside the closure, or chunk metadata that disagrees with the pack footers, is refused, and an adapter that reports zero `estimated_new_bytes` cannot write past the tenant's quota.
- A supervisor replaced by a hostile responder by a helper run or a setup command of the slot's first bound session, before that session's launch, or by any later slot code, gets every hostile reply of S6 refused, and the one it can pass, a descriptor for another file of the same workspace, changes only that session's snapshot.
- A slot cannot make a checkpoint, a restore, or an export of its own session read or write another slot's files, the adapter's files, or `lenny-spool`.
- A snapshot that references a platform pack of a store the session is withheld from, or of another runtime's assets, is refused with `ReferenceRefused`.
- A pod to which a session has bound, on any slot, is refused every grant on `CH-SOURCECHUNKS`, and so is a pod that names a source outside its warm list and its runtime's assets.
- The adapter's contribution reads no file in the store volume: it reads only the staged trees through each slot's broker and the asset files.
- A session's `Checkpoint` and `ExportOutputFile` streams receive no grant under a platform chunk set's prefix.
- A platform pack whose bytes do not match its chunk IDs fails the restore that reads it under S4.

### Tier 11: documentation

- Runbooks for `CheckpointDurationHigh` and `CheckpointStale` describe the pause metric and the convergence fallback.
- The checkpointing page's settings, defaults, and metric names match the configuration reference and the metrics reference.
- The configuration reference lists `gateway.checkpointConcurrency` and `controller.adapterRestoreConcurrency` with the defaults the chart's `values.yaml` sets.

## Decisions

| ID | Decision | Outcome | Alternative |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| D1 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, after asking for cross-session deduplication as an optimization, choosing "part A only", and then asking "would it make sense to follow a hybrid design that uses the foundation from the original idea you proposed, where the only files that would be deduplicated are shared assets and staged git clones? Forget about keying by (store, commit)", with "1. ok" and "2. ok" to the two follow-up questions: deduplication scope. | The allowed set stays per session, and a snapshot may also reference the chunks of platform chunk sets: one set per warm repository store, from the working trees of its warm-up checkouts without `.git`, and one per runtime's shared assets, matched by chunk ID with no record of where a file came from. Four conditions hold it: only a never-launched pod, one to which no session has bound (SIR-38), adds chunks, before READY; the gateway computes each session's eligible sources at every checkpoint and export from its `coveredStores` and its runtime; a platform pack holds one source's chunks; and the reference closure is the allowed set plus the eligible sources' packs (IC-4, "Platform chunk sets"). The answers to the follow-up questions: a tenant whose `encryption.tenantKey` is `required` may reference operator-source chunks stored under the deployment key, and `securitySettings.storage.platformChunkReuse` is `false` in the `regulated` template and `true` in `standard` and `confidential`. A correction after the write-up, on 2026-11-13, narrows eligibility to the contributions that describe the content on the session's own pod, the store's commit at READY and the asset revision the session's runtime revision mounts, and has the gateway answer chunk questions only to a contributing pod that no session has bound, which closes the probe of content removed from a repository's history or from a newer asset revision (IC-4, "Eligibility"). | Session-only scope, as revision 14 recommended, which uploads a large repository's working tree at every session's first checkpoint. Sharing chunks between sessions across a tenant or a user, which a session could probe through the pod's shared network byte counters, checkpoint timing, or the quota on every isolation profile, and which a compromised adapter could exploit by naming a chunk it does not hold unless a trusted verifier and proofs of possession were added. Baselines keyed by store and commit, which the owner replaced with one set per source. |
| D2 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the words "D2 ok (derive references source packs)": derive storage. | A reference to the source's packs, with reference-based garbage collection (IC-5, "Derive"). | Copy every pack, as derive copies tar chunks today. The cost grows with workspace size. |
| D3 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13: the default `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`. The owner accepted "10 GiB default `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`" and, after asking what the change to `ephemeral-storage` involves, "ok (controller renders per-volume sizeLimits and ephemeral-storage request = sum; §17.8.2 quotas and §10.1 drain tiers recomputed)". | 10 GiB on every pool that does not set it. The pod controller renders each disk-backed volume's `sizeLimit` and an `ephemeral-storage` request equal to their sum, 27.25 GiB for a one-slot pod at the defaults before the warm stores, to which decision D21 adds the resource class's own 1 GiB. The §17.8.2 ResourceQuota defaults for `requests.ephemeral-storage` become 6Ti, 70Ti, and 400Ti, which decision D21 raises to 6Ti, 70Ti, and 420Ti, and the §10.1.7 drain tiers, whose last tier ends at 512 MiB, become `evictionCheckpointCapSeconds` (IC-7). | Keep 512 MiB. Large clones then keep failing. |
| D4 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the words "D4 ok (remove partial-manifest reassembly)": partial-manifest reassembly. | Remove it (IC-6, "Partial manifests"). | Keep a pack-level partial snapshot. The design reuses confirmed packs instead. |
| D5 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the words "D5 ok (runtime `checkpointExcludes`)": exclusions. | A runtime-declared `checkpointExcludes` list for regenerable directories such as `node_modules/`, empty by default, with a tenant's entries appended on a derived runtime (IC-8). | No exclusions, or honor a workspace `.lennyignore`, which lets the agent choose what survives a restore. |
| D6 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13: git maintenance. The owner answered "D6 ok", asked whether a runtime can override the settings, and accepted "runtime may override via global/repo/GIT_CONFIG_COUNT; documented". | `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false` in the git configuration `netagent` renders, which `0127-egress-and-git` adopted as its decision 59. The file is git's system scope, so a runtime may override it in its global or repository configuration or with `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, and the cost, a history upload at the next checkpoint after a repack, falls on that session alone. The runtime author guide states it (IC-1, "Git repositories"). | Leave maintenance on and accept a history re-upload after each repack. |
| D7 | Pause budget defaults | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-06: a 10 s pause budget. The spool cap stays at 512 MiB and the pre-copy rounds at 3, followed by best-effort capture. Admission rejects a budget of 60 s or more. | A 5 s budget, which revision 3's first draft recommended. It halves the longest interrupt delay a checkpoint causes, and it sends Chromium-sized workspaces under gVisor without a tracker to the best-effort path. |
| D8 | `checkpointPolicy` values and resolution | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-05: `full`, `sealOnly`, and `none` on the Runtime and the pool, with the stricter value winning. | None. |
| D9 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the words "the pool's value wins (clamped to deployer bounds)": interval resolution between the Runtime and the pool. | The pool's value wins, clamped to the deployer's bounds (IC-8, "Interval"). | The shorter of the two values wins. That protects more of the runtime's work, and it lets a runtime author raise a pool's storage and gateway load without the operator's consent. |
| D10 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the words "D10 ok (tracker as designed, `auto`)": the change source for the capture. | An inotify change tracker, whose instance the slot's supervisor holds under SI decision D9, with the stat walk as its fallback, `checkpointChangeTracker: auto` by default, which runs the tracker only on slot file systems where it is known to see every change: a node file system under runc, gVisor's pod-share mount, a Kata block volume, and Kata's shared file system. Measured steady pause through the broker: 0.013 to 0.094 s on every one of them. | The stat walk only. The pause then grows with file count, to 6.33 s for Chromium on gVisor's default mount and 9.44 s on Kata's shared file system through the broker. A fanotify tracker with file system marks needs `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`, which agent pods do not hold. |
| D11 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13: how the adapter decides to pause. The owner asked to "be a bit conservative" and then accepted "slowest observed rates, pause only if 1.5 x estimate <= budget". | An estimated capture time, decided before `checkpoint_request` from the slowest per-file cost, read rate, clone cost, and change-source cost the adapter has observed for the slot in the attempt's rounds and the session's earlier captures on the pod, with `pauseEstimateMargin`, a constant of 1.5: the adapter pauses only when 1.5 times the estimate is at most the budget. `checkpointCaptureBudgetBytes` stays as the spool cap (IC-1, "Pause budget and convergence"). | A byte budget, as in revision 2, which misses captures of many small files, which cost 50 to 130 µs each on gVisor and Kata's shared file system. The estimate from the latest rates with no margin, as revisions 3 to 15 had it, which pauses more often and overruns the budget more often. |
| D12 | Basic-level checkpoints | Basic runtimes get the best-effort snapshot that §4.4.3 states, with no pause, and §15.4.3, §28.5.3, and the runtime author guide change to match. The adapter needs nothing from the runtime for a best-effort capture. Decision D3 of `0139-runtime-protocol`, settled by the owner, replaces the levels with capabilities, under which every runtime that does not declare `checkpoint` gets this snapshot, so the levels this decision names no longer exist. | Basic runtimes get no checkpoint, as §15.4.3 states. A pod failure then loses the workspace of every Basic session, and credential rotation restarts them without a workspace. |
| D13 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the words "yes" and "we must document all the relevant details of this design (and all others)": deployer guidance and documentation. | The pod controller renders the gVisor pod-share mount hints on every slot workspace and session volume and on `lenny-spool` of pods of pools with `isolationProfile: sandboxed`, and the guide tells deployers to pass `dev.gvisor.*` annotations to runsc. Recommend `emptydir_mode = "block-plain"` with the pool's dedicated `fsGroup` and the guest watch limit on Kata nodes, and the inotify sysctls on runc nodes. State that `--file-access-mounts=exclusive` must not be set and that `--platform=kvm` slows checkpoints. List `virtio_fs_cache = "metadata"` for Kata deployers who keep the shared file system. Beyond node guidance, the documentation lists every setting, default, node requirement, limit, failure mode, and runtime-facing rule of this design ("Changes to Lenny's documentation", item 8). | Leave node configuration out of Lenny's documentation. Deployers on gVisor and Kata then run with the walk pause or a degraded tracker without knowing why. |
| D14 | Where the checkpoint settings live | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-08, in the word "ok": `packMaxBytes` (32 MiB), `racyWindow` (2 s), `bestEffortRereads` (2), `reflinkMinBytes` (1 MiB), and `packRewriteThreshold` (0.5) are constants that the specification states, and the gateway refuses a pack grant larger than `packMaxBytes`. `checkpointConcurrency` (16) is a gateway setting from the chart value `gateway.checkpointConcurrency` with a §17.8.1 row. `restoreConcurrency` (8) is an adapter setting from the chart value `controller.adapterRestoreConcurrency`, which the controller passes to every agent pod. | Make every setting configurable. A configurable `packMaxBytes` changes the bound in S5 per deployment, and the other constants change the pack format or the capture's correctness rules without a measured need. |
| D15 | The embedded `SIGSTOP` checkpoint path | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-09: remove it, with `checkpointStuck` and the liveness integration built on it, as IC-9 states. An embedded binary is checkpointed cooperatively or on the best-effort path. | Keep the path and its liveness rule. The platform would then pause an embedded agent by force, which the owner ruled out, and a node-side freeze of an embedded container under quarantine would fail the liveness probe, so [`0128-forensics-evidence-preservation`](../0128-forensics-evidence-preservation/change.md) would have to amend the rule. |
| D16 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-12, in the words "D16: adopt `statat`": an integer-only `statat` operation of the file-descriptor broker, which returns for up to 253 paths the fields of file identity, the link count, and the type as integers, with no descriptors. | Adopted. On AWS the walk through `open` added 71% to Chromium's pause under runc and put it at 17.2 s on gVisor's default mount, which met both of the adoption criteria revision 13 set, and `statat` cut the walk by 10 to 80% on every runtime except Kata `block-plain`. `statat` alone did not keep Chromium within the budget on gVisor's default mount or Kata's shared file system, so the parallel stat walk of decision D19 uses it as its fallback for directories the adapter may not search. Its answer carries no descriptor, so the adapter cannot check it as S6 checks a descriptor, and a false answer can only leave that session's snapshot stale, as the one reply S6 accepts can leave it wrong. | Keep the broker's operations without `statat` and rely on the tracker to keep the walk out of the pause. A slot without a tracker, such as one on gVisor's default mount, then pays the broker's per-entry cost in every pause, and the parallel stat walk would fall back to broker `open` and `openpath`. |
| D17 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-12, in the words "D17: keep the tracker off on 9p under auto" and "The tracker can be enabled on Kata virtio-fs": the change tracker on gVisor's default mount and on Kata's shared file system. | Under `auto` the tracker stays off on `9p`. At containerd's default `LimitNOFILE` of 65,535, Chromium's 47,106 watches drove the sentry to its descriptor limit, every open in the sandbox then failed with `EMFILE`, and nothing reported an error, which a consistent capture cannot accept (R11). At 1,048,576 the tracker worked with 0 missed changes, and a pod with several slots shares one sandbox, so its slots' watches add up. The pod-share hint stays the way to run the tracker on gVisor. Under `auto` the tracker runs on `virtiofs`, where the supervisor-held tracker missed no change on AWS in any scenario. | Run the tracker on `9p` and require deployers to raise `LimitNOFILE` for the gVisor handler, or run it on `9p` only for slots whose directory count fits a share of the sandbox's descriptor limit, which the adapter cannot read from inside the sandbox. |
| D18 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-12, in the words "D18: copy-mode freeze for files within the export share": how an output file on a slot volume in copy mode is frozen. | Freeze it with a `copy_file_range` copy into `/adapter/spool/<n>/export/{adapter_id}` when the file fits the remaining export share, counted at full size, as reflink mode freezes with a clone, and read it in place with the identity check otherwise. On AWS a 256 MiB copy took 0.056 s on gVisor's pod-share mount, and an in-place export of a file the runtime rewrote failed with `file_changed`. `0135-message-delivery` has to state the change (see "Changes requested of other designs"). | Keep MD-18's rule, under which only reflink mode freezes with a clone and every other slot volume is read in place. |
| D19 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-12 as option (b): how the stat walk reaches a slot's entries quickly, with "a specific concern about directories holding very large numbers of files and about timeouts". | The adapter lists each directory through a broker-passed descriptor and calls `statx` on each name in parallel relative to it, with the name rule, the pinned device and mount ID on every result, `openat2` with the broker's flags for subdirectories, and a per-directory fallback to broker `statat` and `open` on `EACCES` with capped concurrency, under the large-directory rules of IC-1, "The walk". It needs no C change and about 330 lines of Go in the adapter. On AWS it brought the walk to 1.0 to 1.2 times the adapter's direct walk on every runtime, with one broker request per walk, 0 missed changes, and 0 identity differences. R1 still holds only with the tracker, Kata's shared file system sits at the edge of the budget for Chromium, and a flat directory of 1,000,000 entries falls to best effort on gVisor's default mount and on Kata's shared file system. | (a) A combined per-directory operation in the supervisor that returns stats for a whole directory, with names from the adapter's own `getdents`, which adds C and keeps the supervisor's one thread on the walk path. (c) Several broker worker processes forked by the supervisor, each on its own connection, which the local spike measured no faster than `statat` and which adds C. |
| D20 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-12: "stagger checkpoints across slots: at most one slot checkpointing at a time per pod, the others queued", as a decision of this design with a measurement later. The owner accepted the writer's defaults the same day: "spool sizeLimit 2×, fallback concurrency 2, 16 walker threads, stagger covers the whole attempt, restores not queued", and asked for a test on pods with several runtimes that covers file copying both ways, git stores and clones, staggering, and volume and memory sizing, planned once the designs settle. | The adapter runs at most one checkpoint attempt per pod at a time and queues the others in arrival order, with the wait charged to each attempt's deadline (IC-1, "Staggering across slots"). On AWS two slots walking together in one gVisor sandbox slowed each other's pauses by 1.4 to 1.8 times. The queue covers the whole attempt, the seal included, and restores are not queued. The measurement owed under "Validation owed" checks each slot's pause and the time until every slot holds a fresh snapshot on pods of 2 and 4 slots, and the cost of concurrent restores on one pod. | Let slots checkpoint concurrently, as revision 13 did. Each slot's snapshot is fresh sooner, and each slot's pause grows with the number of slots walking at once. |
| D21 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the answers to the consolidated list of open decisions: "yes ~1 GiB", and, asked whether it is configurable, the answer that it is a field of each resource class. The billing design's BR-Q1 is settled as (a). | Each resource class carries its own `requests.ephemeral-storage`, 1 GiB in the shipped classes and configurable per class, added once per pod on top of the sum of the disk-backed `sizeLimit` values ([PL-5](../0118-agent-pod-layout/change.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction), PL decision D18). A one-slot pod requests 28.25 GiB at the defaults before the stores, and the §17.8.2 ResourceQuota defaults become 6Ti, 70Ti, and 420Ti for tiers 1 to 3 (IC-7). PL's decision D27 of 2026-11-14 raises the session volume's default to 1 GiB, so IC-7 now states 29 GiB and 6Ti, 80Ti, and 430Ti. | The request equals the volume sum alone, which reserves nothing for the containers' writable layers and logs. |
| D22 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the words "keep 16 GiB": the spool's default and the capture budget. | `spoolSizeLimitBytes` stays 16 GiB by default, and the 512 MiB per slot is the pool's `checkpointCaptureBudgetBytes`, configurable per pool, as IC-7 states. No rule changes. | A smaller spool default. |
| D23 | Settled by the owner on 2026-11-13, in the word "ok": whether a checkpoint's walk replaces the disk watch's recalibration walk. | It does not. The checkpoint's walk skips the reserved names and the `checkpointExcludes` subtrees, whose bytes the disk watch counts, so the watch keeps its own recalibration walk (IC-1, "Change tracker"; PL decision D21). | Reuse the sum of a checkpoint's walk in place of a recalibration walk. |

## Not verified

- A quiescing runtime's own quiesce time. Both harnesses used `SIGSTOP` on the workload as a stand-in.
- Restore through the gateway's grant protocol and presigned URLs. Both harnesses restored with direct SDK access.
- Clouds other than AWS, Arm nodes, and Kubernetes node images other than Amazon Linux 2023. The Kubernetes checks and the re-validation ran on single-node k3s clusters with kubelet 1.37.
- Whether Kubernetes user namespaces give each pod its own inotify budget under runc, as the kernel's per-user accounting implies.
- Which managed gVisor offerings pass `dev.gvisor.*` pod annotations to runsc. Where they do not, pools with `isolationProfile: sandboxed` keep the walk.
- The earliest gVisor release that serves the broker. `release-20261109.0` is the one measured.
- The parallel stat walk against hostile directory renames, deletions, and symbolic link swaps on AWS. The local spike tested them.
- The `/session` tree in any measurement. The harnesses captured one tree per slot.
- `FICLONE` from a slot volume to `lenny-spool` on a kernel before 5.18, which compares mounts and returns `EXDEV`. The reflink probe then finds copy or read mode.
- Kata `block-plain` with more than one workspace volume per pod, and its behavior when the node's disk fills, since `disk.img` is sparse.
- Ranged `GET` requests on presigned URLs for each backend, which would let a restore skip unused chunks of a pack. This design fetches whole packs.
- Whether `Content-MD5` binds into the signature on each backend. It does on SigV4 (AWS Signature Version 4) backends that sign it. An Azure SAS (shared access signature) does not bind request headers, so on Azure the write-time check depends on the adapter, and S4 is the control.
- Timestamp granularity on the node kernels Lenny targets. The constant `racyWindow` is set conservatively.
- Which local CSI drivers provide `VolumeSnapshot` on ZFS or Btrfs, for the deferred node-level provider.

The AWS re-validation of 2026-11-12 settled items that revision 13 listed here: the broker's cost on AWS, on x86, and on Kata, `copy_file_range` from a broker-passed descriptor under gVisor, the supervisor-held tracker under load, the broker's serving of several adapter threads, which it serializes on one thread, and the cost of the identity pass after a restore, which its restores included. The Kubernetes checks settled three items that revision 3's first draft listed here: inotify across containers, `--file-access-mounts=exclusive` against kubelet-updated volumes, and `block-plain` against the `emptyDir` size limit. The cloud runs settled items that revision 2 listed here: `FICLONE` is available under runc only, the stat walk's cost under gVisor and Kata is in "Benchmarks", and zstd's fastest level compresses at 0.85 to 2.0 GiB/s per core. Cold reads of small files ran at 17 to 38 MiB/s on gp3 and at 154 to 351 MiB/s on local NVMe, and the capture read recently changed files from the page cache in every scenario.
